## Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act {#federal-public-sector-labour-relations-act .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 2003, c. 22, s. 2
:::

Assented to 2003-11-07

An Act respecting labour relations in the federal public sector

::: ReaderNote
\[Enacted by section 2 of chapter 22 of the Statutes of Canada, 2003;
preamble, sections 1 to 3 and Part 1, in force April 1, 2005, see
SI/2005-22; Part 2, other than subparagraph 209(1)(c)(ii), paragraph
211(b) and section 231, in force April 1, 2005, see SI/2005-23; Parts 3
and 4 in force April 1, 2005, see SI/2005-24; subparagraph
209(1)(c)(ii), paragraph 211(b) and section 231 in force December 31,
2005, see SI/2005-123.\]
:::
:::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
###### Preamble {#preamble .MarginalNote}

Recognizing that

the public service labour-management regime must operate in a context
where protection of the public interest is paramount;

effective labour-management relations represent a cornerstone of good
human resource management and that collaborative efforts between the
parties, through communication and sustained dialogue, improve the
ability of the public service to serve and protect the public interest;

collective bargaining ensures the expression of diverse views for the
purpose of establishing terms and conditions of employment;

the Government of Canada is committed to fair, credible and efficient
resolution of matters arising in respect of terms and conditions of
employment;

the Government of Canada recognizes that public service bargaining
agents represent the interests of employees in collective bargaining and
participate in the resolution of workplace issues and rights disputes;

commitment from the employer and bargaining agents to mutual respect and
harmonious labour-management relations is essential to a productive and
effective public service;
:::

::: Enacts
NOW, THEREFORE, Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the
Senate and House of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-404907 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "1"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-1-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 1"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-404912 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this Act.

  [[adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person or board of
      adjudication to whom a grievance is referred under paragraph
      223(2)(a), (b) or (c). ([arbitre de grief]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[arbitral award]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[arbitral award]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an award made by an
      arbitration board in respect of a dispute. ([décision
      arbitrale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[arbitration board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[arbitration board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a board
      established under Division 9 of Part 1. ([conseil
      d'arbitrage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[bargaining agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bargaining agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee
      organization that is certified by the Board as the bargaining
      agent for the employees in a bargaining unit. ([agent
      négociateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[bargaining unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bargaining unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a group of two or
      more employees that is determined by the Board to constitute a
      unit of employees appropriate for collective bargaining. ([unité
      de négociation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Federal Public Sector
      Labour Relations and Employment Board referred to in subsection
      4(1) of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and Employment
      Board Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35). ([Commission]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chairperson of the
      Board. ([président]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement in
      writing, entered into under Part 1 between the employer and a
      bargaining agent, containing provisions respecting terms and
      conditions of employment and related matters. ([convention
      collective]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[core public administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[core public administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in subsection 11(1) of the [Financial Administration
      Act](/eng/acts/F-11). ([administration publique
      centrale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[council of employee organizations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[council of employee organizations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      council formed by two or more employee organizations within the
      meaning of paragraph (a) of the definition [[employee
      organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or by two or more employee
      organizations within the meaning of paragraph (b) of that
      definition. ([regroupement d'organisations
      syndicales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[deputy head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[deputy head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a deputy head referred
      to in any of paragraphs (a) to (c) of the definition [[deputy
      head]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 11(1) of the [Financial
      Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11). ([administrateur
      général]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[dispute]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[dispute]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dispute or difference that
      arises in connection with the entering into, renewal or revision
      of a collective agreement and in respect of which arbitration may
      be requested under subsection 136(1) or conciliation may be
      requested under subsection 161(1). ([différend]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, except in Part 2, means a person
      employed in the public service, other than

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person appointed by the Governor in Council
        under an Act of Parliament to a statutory position described in
        that Act;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person locally engaged outside Canada;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person not ordinarily required to work more
        than one third of the normal period for persons doing similar
        work;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who is an
        [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1)
        of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10);

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person employed in the Canadian Security
        Intelligence Service who does not perform duties of a clerical
        or secretarial nature;

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person employed on a casual basis;

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person employed on a term basis, unless the
        term of employment is for a period of three months or more or
        the person has been so employed for a period of three months or
        more;

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an employee of the Administrative Tribunals
        Support Service of Canada who provides any of the following
        services exclusively to the Board:

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} mediation and dispute resolution services,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} legal services,

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} advisory services relating to the Board's
          exercise of its powers and performance of its duties and
          functions;

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person who occupies a managerial or
        confidential position; or

      - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a person who is employed under a program
        designated by the employer as a student employment
        program. ([fonctionnaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[employee organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employee organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of employees who are not RCMP
        members or reservists, an organization of employees that has as
        one of its purposes the regulation of relations between the
        employer and its employees for the purposes of Parts 1 and 2;
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of employees who are RCMP members or
        reservists, an organization of those employees that has as one
        of its purposes the regulation of relations between the employer
        and its employees for the purposes of Parts 1, 2 and
        2.1. ([organisation syndicale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Her Majesty in right of
      Canada as represented by

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Treasury Board, in the case of a department
        named in Schedule I to the [Financial Administration
        Act](/eng/acts/F-11) or another portion of the federal public
        administration named in Schedule IV to that Act; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the separate agency, in the case of a portion
        of the federal public administration named in Schedule V to the
        [Financial Administration
        Act](/eng/acts/F-11). ([employeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[managerial or confidential position]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[managerial or confidential position]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
      a position declared to be a managerial or confidential position by
      an order made by the Board under subsection 62(1), section 63,
      subsection 74(1) or section 75. ([poste de direction ou de
      confiance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a member of the Board,
      whether full-time or part-time. ([commissaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[membership dues]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[membership dues]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of employees
      represented by a bargaining agent, means the amount that the
      employer is required to deduct from the pay of the employees and
      remit to the bargaining agent under any collective agreement that
      is entered into between the employer and the bargaining
      agent. ([cotisations syndicales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the member of the Queen's
      Privy Council for Canada, other than a member of the Treasury
      Board, designated by the Governor in Council as the Minister for
      the purposes of this Act. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[National Joint Council]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[National Joint Council]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National
      Joint Council whose establishment was authorized by Order in
      Council P.C. 3676, dated May 16, 1944. ([Conseil national
      mixte]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[public service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[public service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, except in Part 3, means
      the several positions in or under

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the departments named in Schedule I to the
        [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11);

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the other portions of the federal public
        administration named in Schedule IV to that Act; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the separate agencies named in Schedule V to
        that Act. ([fonction publique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[RCMP member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[RCMP member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, except in Division 2 of Part
      2.1, means a [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in
      subsection 2(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
      Act](/eng/acts/R-10) who is appointed to a rank. ([membre de la
      GRC]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[reservist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[reservist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is appointed
      as a reservist under regulations made under subsection 11(1) of
      the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
      Act](/eng/acts/R-10). ([réserviste]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[separate agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[separate agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      subsection 11(1) of the [Financial Administration
      Act](/eng/acts/F-11). ([organisme distinct]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[strike]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[strike]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a cessation of work or a
      refusal to work or to continue to work by persons employed in the
      public service, in combination, in concert or in accordance with a
      common understanding, and a slow-down of work or any other
      concerted activity on the part of such persons that is designed to
      restrict or limit output. ([grève]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Vice-Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Vice-Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a Vice-Chairperson
      of the Board. ([vice-président]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employment status preserved

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person does not cease to be employed in the public
  service by reason only that the person ceases to work as a result of a
  strike or by reason only of the termination of the person's employment
  contrary to this Act or any other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons who are not employees

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a person is not an employee if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is engaged under section 30 of the
    [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and Employment Board
    Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person's compensation for the performance of
    the regular duties of the person's position or office consists of
    fees of office or is related to the revenue of the office in which
    the person is employed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Casual employment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (f) of the definition
  [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection (1) and paragraph (e)
  of the definition [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection
  206(1), a person employed in the part of the public service to which
  the Public Service Commission has the exclusive right to make
  appointments is employed on a casual basis if the person was appointed
  under section 50 of the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to occupants of positions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every reference to a person who occupies a position,
  or to the occupant of a position, includes a person who is acting in
  that position or who has assumed wholly or substantially the duties
  and responsibilities of that position, and a reference to a person's
  position includes the position of a person who is acting in that
  position or who has assumed wholly or substantially the duties and
  responsibilities of that position.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to employee organization

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Unless the context otherwise requires, a reference to
  an employee organization includes a reference to a council of employee
  organizations, and a reference to an employee organization within the
  meaning of paragraph (a) or (b), as the case may be, of the definition
  [[employee organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1)
  includes a reference to a council formed by two or more employee
  organizations within the meaning of that paragraph.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 2 "2", 243
- 2013, c. 40, s. 366
- 2014, c. 20, ss. 472, 481
- 2017, c. 9, ss. 3, 56
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Descriptive cross- references

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** If, in any provision of
this Act, a reference to another provision of this Act is followed by
words in parentheses that are descriptive of the subject-matter of that
other provision, the words in parentheses form no part of the provision
in which they occur and are deemed to have been inserted for convenience
of reference only.

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Labour Relations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-404978 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-404979 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this Part.

  [[essential service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[essential service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a service,
      facility or activity of the Government of Canada that is or will
      be, at any time, necessary for the safety or security of the
      public or a segment of the public. ([services
      essentiels]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[essential services agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[essential services agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
      agreement between the employer and the bargaining agent for a
      bargaining unit that identifies 

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the types of positions in the bargaining unit
        that are necessary for the employer to provide essential
        services;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of those positions that are
        necessary for that purpose; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the specific positions that are necessary for
        that purpose. ([entente sur les services
        essentiels]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[mediator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[mediator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person appointed as a
      mediator under subsection 108(1). ([médiateur]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[National Joint Council]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[National Joint Council]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      2017, c. 9, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

  [[parties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to collective
      bargaining, arbitration, conciliation or a dispute, means the
      employer and the bargaining agent. ([parties]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[public interest commission]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[public interest commission]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      commission established under Division 10. ([commission de
      l'intérêt public]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When position is necessary

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A position that is necessary for the employer to
  provide essential services for the purposes of paragraph (a) of the
  definition [[essential services agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
  subsection (1) includes a position the occupant of which is required,
  at any time,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to perform the duties of the position that relate
    to the provision of essential services; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to be available during his or her off-duty hours to
    report to work without delay to perform those duties if required to
    do so by the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "4"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 294
- 2017, c. 9, s. 4
- 2018, c. 24, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Employee Freedoms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-404998 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee freedoms

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** Every employee is free to
join the employee organization of his or her choice and to participate
in its lawful activities.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Management Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405001 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of Treasury Board preserved

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this Act is to
be construed as affecting the right or authority of the Treasury Board
under paragraph 7(1)(b) of the [Financial Administration
Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of employer preserved

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this Act is to
be construed as affecting the right or authority of the Treasury Board
or a separate agency to determine the organization of those portions of
the federal public administration for which it represents Her Majesty in
right of Canada as employer or to assign duties to and to classify
positions and persons employed in those portions of the federal public
administration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of Commissioner of Royal Canadian
Mounted Police preserved

**[[7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.1 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this Act is
to be construed as affecting the right or authority of the Commissioner
of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police under the [Royal Canadian Mounted
Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) to ensure that police operations are
effective.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 4.1
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Consultation Committees and Co-Development]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405010 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation committee

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** Each deputy head must, in
consultation with the bargaining agents representing employees in the
portion of the federal public administration for which he or she is
deputy head, establish a consultation committee consisting of
representatives of the deputy head and the bargaining agents for the
purpose of exchanging information and obtaining views and advice on
issues relating to the workplace that affect those employees, which
issues may include, among other things,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} harassment in the workplace; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of information concerning wrongdoing
  in the public service and the protection from reprisal of employees
  who disclose such information.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of [[co-development of workplace
improvements]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of this
Division, [[co-development of workplace
improvements]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the consultation between the
parties on workplace issues and their participation in the
identification of workplace problems and the development and analysis of
solutions to those problems with a view to adopting mutually agreed to
solutions.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Co-development of workplace improvements

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** The employer and a
bargaining agent, or a deputy head and a bargaining agent, may engage in
co-development of workplace improvements.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}National Joint Council

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Co-development of workplace
  improvements by the employer and a bargaining agent may take place
  under the auspices of the National Joint Council or any other body
  they may agree on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facilities and administrative support

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Administrator of the Administrative
  Tribunals Support Service of Canada is to provide facilities and
  administrative support to the National Joint Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "11"
- 2014, c. 20, s. 481
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and Employment Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405026 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administration of Act

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** The Board administers
this Act and it may exercise the powers and perform the duties and
functions that are conferred or imposed on it by this Act, or as are
incidental to the attainment of the objects of this Act, including the
making of orders requiring compliance with this Act, with regulations
made under it or with decisions made in respect of a matter coming
before the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "12"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 367
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjudication services

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** The Board is to provide
adjudication services that consist of the hearing of applications and
complaints made under this Part and Division 1 of Part 2.1, the referral
of grievances to adjudication in accordance with Part 2 and Division 2
of Part 2.1 and the hearing of matters brought before the Board under
Part 3.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "13"
- 2013, c. 40, ss. 295, 367
- 2017, c. 9, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediation services

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** The Board is to provide
mediation services that consist of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} assisting parties in the negotiation of collective
  agreements and their renewal;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} assisting parties in the management of the relations
  resulting from the implementation of collective agreements;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} mediating in relation to grievances; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} assisting the Chairperson in discharging his or her
  responsibilities under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "14"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 367
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2014, c. 20,
s. 481\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** The Board has, in
relation to any matter before it, the power to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} examine any evidence that is submitted to it
  respecting membership of employees in an employee organization seeking
  certification and, in the case of a council of employee organizations
  seeking certification, in any employee organization forming part of
  the council;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} examine documents forming or relating to the
  constitution or articles of association of any employee organization
  seeking certification and, in the case of a council of employee
  organizations seeking certification, those of any employee
  organization forming part of the council;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} require the employer to post and keep posted in
  appropriate places any notice that the Board considers necessary to
  bring matters or proceedings before the Board to the attention of
  employees;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to any limitations that the Governor in
  Council may establish in the interests of defence or security, enter
  any premises of the employer where work is being or has been done by
  employees, inspect and view any work, material, machinery, appliance
  or article in the premises and require any person in the premises to
  answer all questions relating to the matter before it;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} subject to any limitations that the Governor in
  Council may establish in the interests of defence or security, enter
  any premises of the employer for the purpose of conducting
  representation votes during working hours; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} authorize any person to do anything that the Board
  may do under paragraphs (a) to (e) and require the person to report to
  it on what the person has done.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "16"
- 2013, c. 40, ss. 296, 367
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to make regulations

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** The Board may make
regulations concerning

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the certification of bargaining agents for bargaining
  units;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the determination of units appropriate for collective
  bargaining;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the time and manner of making applications under
  section 59, the provision of copies of those applications and the
  filing of objections in respect of any positions referred to in those
  applications;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the authority vested in a council of employee
  organizations that is to be considered the appropriate authority
  within the meaning of paragraph 64(1)(c);

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manner of making applications under sections 71
  and 77, the time and manner of providing copies of those applications
  and the time and manner of the filing of objections in respect of any
  positions referred to in applications under section 71;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the rights, privileges and duties that are acquired
  or retained by an employee organization in respect of a bargaining
  unit or any employee included in a bargaining unit when there is a
  merger, an amalgamation or a transfer of jurisdiction between two or
  more employee organizations;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the revocation of certification of a bargaining
  agent, including the rights and privileges that have accrued to and
  are retained by any employee despite the revocation;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the manner of giving notices referred to in
  subsection 103(1), and the form of those notices, and the manner of
  making applications referred to in subsection 104(1), and the form of
  those applications;

- [(i) to (k)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2013, c. 40, s. 368\]]{.Repealed}

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which the following evidence may
  be received by it as evidence that any employees wish or do not wish
  to have a particular employee organization represent them as their
  bargaining agent, and the circumstances in which it must not make
  public any evidence so received:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} evidence as to membership of employees in an
    employee organization,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} evidence of objection by employees to
    certification of an employee organization, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} evidence of signification by employees that they
    no longer wish to be represented by an employee organization; and

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} any other matter that is incidental or conducive to
  the attainment of the objects of this Part or Division 1 of Part 2.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "39"
- 2013, c. 40, ss. 297, 368
- 2017, c. 9, s. 6
- 2018, c. 24, s. 2
:::

Version](section-39-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 39"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of orders

**[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** In making an order or a
decision, or doing any other thing in relation to any person under this
Act, the Board may do so either generally or in any particular case or
class of cases.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of orders and decisions

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the Board
  may review, rescind or amend any of its orders or decisions, or may
  re-hear any application before making an order in respect of the
  application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A right that is acquired by virtue of an order or a
  decision that is reviewed, rescinded or amended by the Board may not
  be altered or extinguished with effect from a day that is earlier than
  the day on which the review, rescission or amendment is made.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Bargaining Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405113 .Subheading}

#### [Certification of Bargaining Agents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-405114 .Subheading}

##### [Application for Certification]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405115 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to apply

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 55, an
employee organization within the meaning of paragraph (a) of the
definition [[employee organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection
2(1) that seeks to be certified as bargaining agent for a group of
employees that it considers constitutes a unit that is appropriate for
collective bargaining may apply to the Board, in accordance with the
regulations, for certification as bargaining agent for the proposed
bargaining unit. The Board must notify the employer of the application
without delay.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "54"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-54-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 54"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements for term of two years or less

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  collective agreement, or an arbitral award, with a term of two years
  or less applies in respect of any employees in the proposed bargaining
  unit for which an employee organization is seeking to be certified as
  bargaining agent, the application for certification may be made only
  after the commencement of the last two months of its term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements for term of more than two
  years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a collective agreement, or an arbitral award, with
  a term of more than two years applies in respect of any employees in
  the proposed bargaining unit for which an employee organization is
  seeking to be certified as bargaining agent, the application for
  certification may be made only

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after the commencement of the twenty-third month of
    its term and before the commencement of the twenty-fifth month of
    its term;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during the two-month period immediately before the
    end of each year that the agreement or award continues to be in
    force after the second year of its term; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} after the commencement of the last two months of
    its term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements for an indefinite term

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a collective agreement that applies in respect of
  any employees in the proposed bargaining unit for which an employee
  organization is seeking to be certified as bargaining agent provides
  that it will continue to operate after the term specified in it for a
  further term or successive terms if either party fails to give to the
  other a notice of termination or a notice of its desire to bargain
  with a view to the renewal of the collective agreement, with or
  without modifications, the application for certification may be made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at any time permitted by subsection (1) or (2), as
    the case may be; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during the two-month period immediately before the
    end of each year that the collective agreement continues to operate
    after the term specified in the collective agreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of terms and conditions

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** After being notified of
an application for certification made in accordance with this Part or
Division 1 of Part 2.1, the employer is not authorized, except under a
collective agreement or with the consent of the Board, to alter the
terms and conditions of employment that are applicable to the employees
in the proposed bargaining unit and that may be included in a collective
agreement until

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application has been withdrawn by the employee
  organization or dismissed by the Board; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 30 days have elapsed after the day on which the Board
  certifies the employee organization as the bargaining agent for the
  unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "56"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-56-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 56"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Determination of Appropriate Bargaining Units]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405138 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of unit

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  an application for certification is made under section 54, the Board
  must determine the group of employees that constitutes a unit
  appropriate for collective bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of employer's
  classification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether a group of employees
  constitutes a unit appropriate for collective bargaining, the Board
  must have regard to the employer's classification of persons and
  positions, including the occupational groups or subgroups established
  by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unit co-extensive with occupational
  groups

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board must establish bargaining units that are
  co-extensive with the occupational groups or subgroups established by
  the employer, unless doing so would not permit satisfactory
  representation of the employees to be included in a particular
  bargaining unit and, for that reason, such a unit would not be
  appropriate for collective bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of bargaining unit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, a unit of employees
  may be determined by the Board to constitute a unit appropriate for
  collective bargaining whether or not its composition is identical with
  the group of employees in respect of which the application for
  certification was made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of questions of membership
in bargaining units

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** On application by the
employer or the employee organization affected, the Board must determine
every question that arises as to whether any employee or class of
employees is included in a bargaining unit determined by the Board to
constitute a unit appropriate for collective bargaining, or is included
in any other unit.

##### [Managerial or Confidential Positions]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405150 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After
  being notified of an application for certification made in accordance
  with this Part or Division 1 of Part 2.1, the employer may apply to
  the Board for an order declaring that any position of an employee in
  the proposed bargaining unit is a managerial or confidential position
  on the grounds that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the position is confidential to the Governor
    General, a Minister of the Crown, a judge of the Supreme Court of
    Canada, the Federal Court of Appeal, the Federal Court or the Tax
    Court of Canada, or a deputy head;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the position is classified by the employer as being
    in the executive group, by whatever name called;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the occupant of the position provides advice on
    labour relations, staffing or classification;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the occupant of the position has substantial duties
    and responsibilities in the formulation and determination of any
    policy or program of the Government of Canada;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the occupant of the position has substantial
    management duties, responsibilities and authority over employees or
    has duties and responsibilities dealing formally on behalf of the
    employer with grievances presented in accordance with the grievance
    process provided for under Part 2 or Division 2 of Part 2.1;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the occupant of the position is directly involved
    in the process of collective bargaining on behalf of the employer;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the occupant of the position has duties and
    responsibilities not otherwise described in this subsection and
    should not be included in a bargaining unit for reasons of conflict
    of interest or by reason of the person's duties and responsibilities
    to the employer; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the occupant of the position has, in relation to
    labour relations matters, duties and responsibilities confidential
    to the occupant of a position described in paragraph (b), (c), (d)
    or (f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must set out every position that the
  employer considers to be a position referred to in any of paragraphs
  (1)(a) to (h).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 2 "59", 275
- 2017, c. 9, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-59-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 59"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to employee organization

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** The employer must provide
the employee organization seeking to be certified with a copy of the
application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** If the employee
organization considers that a particular position in the employer's
application is not a position referred to in any of paragraphs 59(1)(a)
to (h), it may file an objection in respect of that position with the
Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision on objection

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  objection is filed in respect of a particular position included in the
  application, the Board must, after giving the employer and the
  employee organization an opportunity to make representations,
  determine whether the position is a position referred to in any of
  paragraphs 59(1)(a) to (h) and, if it determines that it is, make an
  order declaring the position to be a managerial or confidential
  position.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof on employee
  organization

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a particular position is
  not a position referred to in any of paragraphs 59(1)(a) to (c) is on
  the employee organization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof on employer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a particular position is a
  position referred to in any of paragraphs 59(1)(d) to (h) is on the
  employer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When no objection filed

**[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** If no objection is filed
in respect of a particular position included in the application, the
Board must make an order declaring the position to be a managerial or
confidential position.

##### [Certification]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405179 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for certification

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After
  having determined the unit appropriate for collective bargaining, the
  Board must certify the applicant employee organization as the
  bargaining agent for the bargaining unit if the Board is satisfied

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that a majority of employees in that bargaining
    unit wish the applicant employee organization to represent them as
    their bargaining agent;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the persons representing the employee
    organization in the making of the application have been duly
    authorized to make the application; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the applicant is a council of employee
    organizations, that each of the employee organizations forming the
    council has vested appropriate authority in the council to enable it
    to discharge the duties and responsibilities of a bargaining agent.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 12, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where previous application denied
  within six months

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an application for certification of an employee
  organization as the bargaining agent for a proposed bargaining unit
  has been denied by the Board, the Board may not consider a new
  application for certification from that employee organization in
  respect of the same or substantially the same proposed bargaining unit
  until at least six months have elapsed from the day on which the
  employee organization was last denied certification, unless the Board
  is satisfied that the previous application was denied by reason only
  of a technical error or omission made in connection with the
  application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership in council of employee
  organizations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), membership in
  any employee organization that forms part of a council of employee
  organizations is deemed to be membership in the council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "64"

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation vote

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may order that a representation vote be taken among the
  employees in the bargaining unit for the purpose of satisfying itself
  that a majority of them wish the applicant employee organization to
  represent them as their bargaining agent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrangements for vote

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When the Board orders that a representation vote be
  taken, it must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine the employees who are eligible to vote;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make any arrangements and give any directions that
    it considers necessary for the proper conduct of the vote, including
    the preparation of ballots, the method of casting and counting
    ballots and the custody and sealing of ballot boxes.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "65"
- 2014, c. 40, s. 10
- 2017, c. 12, s. 9
:::

Version](section-65-20150616.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Where Certification Prohibited]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405202 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer participation

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may not certify an employee organization as a bargaining agent
  if it is of the opinion that the employer, or a person acting on
  behalf of the employer, has participated or is participating in the
  formation or administration of the employee organization in a manner
  that impairs its fitness to represent the interests of the employees
  in the bargaining unit for which it is proposed to be certified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discrimination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board may not certify an employee organization as
  a bargaining agent if it discriminates against any employee on a
  prohibited ground of discrimination within the meaning of the
  [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

##### [Effect of Certification]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405208 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certification

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** Certification of an
employee organization as the bargaining agent for a bargaining unit has
the following effects:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization has exclusive authority to
  bargain collectively on behalf of the employees in the bargaining
  unit;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the certification of any employee organization that
  was previously certified as the bargaining agent for any employees in
  the bargaining unit is deemed to be revoked to the extent that the
  certification relates to those employees;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization is substituted as a party
  to any collective agreement or arbitral award that affects any
  employees in the bargaining unit, to the extent that the agreement or
  award relates to those employees, in the place of the bargaining agent
  named in the collective agreement or its successor;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization is deemed to be the
  bargaining agent for the purposes of section 107; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization is substituted as a party
  to any essential services agreement that is in force, in the place of
  the bargaining agent named in the agreement or its successor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "67"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 299
- 2018, c. 24, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-67-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 67"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of existing collective
agreement or arbitral award

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** An employee organization
that is certified as the bargaining agent for a bargaining unit may,
despite anything contained in any collective agreement or arbitral award
that is binding on any employees in the bargaining unit on the day of
certification, terminate the agreement or award, in so far as it applies
to the employees in the bargaining unit, on two months' notice to the
employer given within one month from the day of certification.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of previous or new bargaining
agent

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** Any question as to any
right or duty of the previous bargaining agent or the new bargaining
agent arising by reason of the application of paragraph 67(b) or (c) or
section 68 must, on application by the employer or the previous or new
bargaining agent, be determined by the Board.

#### [Changes to Certification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-405222 .Subheading}

##### [Review of Bargaining Units]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405223 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of structure of bargaining units

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  Board reviews the structure of one or more bargaining units, it must,
  in determining whether a group of employees constitutes a unit
  appropriate for collective bargaining, have regard to the employer's
  classification of persons and positions, including the occupational
  groups or subgroups established by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unit co-extensive with occupational
  groups

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board must establish bargaining units that are
  co-extensive with the occupational groups or subgroups established by
  the employer, unless doing so would not permit satisfactory
  representation of the employees to be included in a particular
  bargaining unit and, for that reason, such a unit would not be
  appropriate for collective bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the Board is not permitted to
  review the structure of the bargaining unit determined under section
  238.14.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "70"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 11
:::

[Previous
Version](section-70-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Managerial or Confidential Positions]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405233 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer may apply to the Board for an order declaring that any
  position of an employee in a bargaining unit for which a bargaining
  agent has been certified by the Board is a managerial or confidential
  position on the grounds that the position is a position referred to in
  any of paragraphs 59(1)(a) to (h).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must set out every position the
  employer considers to be a position referred to in any of paragraphs
  59(1)(a) to (h).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to bargaining agent

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** The employer must provide
the bargaining agent with a copy of the application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** If the bargaining agent
considers that a particular position in the employer's application is
not a position referred to in any of paragraphs 59(1)(a) to (h), it may
file an objection in respect of that position with the Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision on objection

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  objection is filed in respect of a particular position included in the
  application, the Board must, after giving the employer and the
  bargaining agent an opportunity to make representations, determine
  whether the position is a position referred to in any paragraphs
  59(1)(a) to (h) and, if it determines that it is, make an order
  declaring the position to be a managerial or confidential position.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof on bargaining agent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a particular position is
  not a position referred to in any of paragraphs 59(1)(a) to (c) is on
  the bargaining agent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof on employer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a particular position is a
  position referred to in any of paragraphs 59(1)(d) to (h) is on the
  employer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When no objection filed

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** If no objection is filed
in respect of a particular position included in the application, the
Board must make an order declaring the position to be a managerial or
confidential position.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership dues

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  objection is filed under section 73, the employer must hold the amount
  that would otherwise be the membership dues in respect of the occupant
  of the position to which the objection relates until the Board makes
  an order declaring the position to be a managerial or confidential
  position, until it dismisses the application in respect of the
  position or until the objection is withdrawn, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remission of dues to occupant of
  position

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board makes an order declaring the position to
  be a managerial or confidential position or the objection is
  withdrawn, the amount held by the employer under subsection (1) must
  be remitted to the person to whom it relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remission of dues to bargaining agent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Board makes an order dismissing the
  application in respect of the position, the amount held by the
  employer under subsection (1) must be remitted to the bargaining
  agent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for revocation of order

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  bargaining agent considers that a position is no longer a managerial
  or confidential position, the bargaining agent may apply to the Board
  for an order revoking the order that declared that position to be a
  managerial or confidential position.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to employer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The bargaining agent must provide the employer with a
  copy of the application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  application is made under section 77, the Board must, after giving the
  employer and the bargaining agent an opportunity to make
  representations, determine whether the position is still a managerial
  or confidential position and, if it determines that it is not, make an
  order revoking the order that declared the position to be a managerial
  or confidential position.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof on bargaining agent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a particular position is
  no longer a managerial or confidential position is on the bargaining
  agent.

#### [Successor Rights and Obligations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-405269 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mergers, amalgamations and transfers of
jurisdiction

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, by
  reason of a merger or an amalgamation of employee organizations or a
  transfer of jurisdiction among employee organizations, other than as a
  result of a revocation of certification, an employee organization
  succeeds another one that, at the time of the merger, amalgamation or
  transfer of jurisdiction, is a bargaining agent, the successor is
  deemed to have acquired the rights, privileges and duties of its
  predecessor, whether under a collective agreement, an arbitral award,
  an essential services agreement or otherwise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board to determine questions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If any question arises in respect of a merger,
  amalgamation or transfer of jurisdiction referred to in subsection (1)
  concerning the rights, privileges and duties of an employee
  organization under this Part or Division 1 of Part 2.1 or under a
  collective agreement, an arbitral award or an essential services
  agreement in respect of a bargaining unit or an employee in a
  bargaining unit, the Board, on application by the employer or any
  person or employee organization concerned, must determine what rights,
  privileges and duties have been acquired or are retained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry and votes

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before making a determination on the application, the
  Board may make any inquiry or direct that a representation vote be
  taken among the employees to be affected by the determination. The
  provisions of subsection 65(2) apply in relation to the taking of the
  vote.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "79"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 300
- 2017, c. 9, s. 12
- 2018, c. 24, s. 4
:::

[Previous
Version](section-79-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this section and sections 81 to 93.

[[conversion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[conversion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the establishment as a
    separate agency, or the integration into a separate agency, of any
    portion, or part of a portion, of the core public
    administration. ([conversion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[new separate agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[new separate agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a separate agency
    established as a result of a conversion or into which is integrated
    any portion, or part of a portion, of the core public administration
    as the result of a conversion. ([nouvel organisme
    distinct]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of collective agreement or
arbitral award

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** Subject to sections 83 to
93, a collective agreement or arbitral award that applies to employees
in any portion, or part of a portion, of the core public administration
before its conversion continues in force after the conversion, and binds
the new separate agency, until its term expires.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties may amend

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in section 81
prohibits the new separate agency and the bargaining agent from amending
any provision of a collective agreement, other than a provision relating
to its term.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for certification

**[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** An employee organization
may apply to the Board for certification as the bargaining agent for the
employees bound by a collective agreement or arbitral award that is
continued in force by section 81, but it may do so only during the
period in which an application for certification is authorized to be
made under section 55 in respect of those employees.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of Board

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Whenever a collective agreement or
  arbitral award is continued in force by section 81, the Board must, by
  order, on application by the new separate agency or any bargaining
  agent affected by the conversion,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine whether the employees of the new separate
    agency who are bound by any collective agreement or arbitral award
    constitute one or more units appropriate for collective bargaining;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determine which employee organization is to be the
    bargaining agent for the employees in each such unit; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each collective agreement or arbitral
    award that binds employees of the new separate agency, determine
    whether the collective agreement or arbitral award is to remain in
    force and, if it is to remain in force, determine whether it is to
    remain in force until the expiration of its term or until any
    earlier date that the Board may fix.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application may be made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application may be made only during the period
  beginning 120 days and ending 150 days after the conversion date.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for leave to give notice to
bargain collectively

- **[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Either
  party to a collective agreement or arbitral award that remains in
  force by reason of an order made under paragraph 84(1)(c) may apply to
  the Board for an order granting leave to give to the other party,
  under section 105, a notice to bargain collectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application may be made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must be made within 90 days after the
  day on which the order is made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for leave to give notice to
bargain collectively

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If no
  application for an order under subsection 84(1) is made within the
  period specified in subsection 84(2), the new separate agency or any
  bargaining agent bound by a collective agreement or arbitral award
  that is continued in force by section 81 may apply to the Board for an
  order granting leave to give to the other party, under section 105, a
  notice to bargain collectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application may be made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application may be made only during the period
  beginning 151 days and ending 240 days after the date of the
  conversion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to bargain given before conversion

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** A notice to bargain
collectively that was given before a conversion does not bind the new
separate agency and a new notice to bargain collectively may be given
only in the circumstances described in paragraph 89(b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to observe terms and conditions

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** If a notice to bargain
collectively was given before a conversion, then, unless the new
separate agency and the bargaining agent agree otherwise, the terms and
conditions of employment continued in force by section 107 are binding
on the new separate agency, the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit
and the employees in the bargaining unit from the date of the conversion
until

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the expiry of 150 days following the date of the
  conversion, if no application is made under paragraph 89(a); or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such an application is made, the day the notice
  referred to in paragraph 89(b) is given.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application and notice to bargain

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** If a notice to bargain
collectively was given before a conversion,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on application by the new separate agency or
  bargaining agent, made during the period beginning 120 days, and
  ending 150 days, after the date of the conversion, the Board must make
  an order determining

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether the employees of the new separate agency
    who are represented by the bargaining agent constitute one or more
    units appropriate for collective bargaining, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} which employee organization is to be the
    bargaining agent for the employees in each such unit; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the Board makes the determinations under paragraph
  (a), the new separate agency or the bargaining agent may, by notice
  given under section 105, require the other to commence collective
  bargaining for the purpose of entering into a collective agreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry and votes

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** Before making an order
under subsection 84(1) or paragraph 89(a), the Board may make any
inquiry or direct that a representation vote be taken among the
employees to be affected by the order. The provisions of subsection
65(2) apply in relation to the taking of a vote.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of employer's
classification

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraphs 84(1)(a) and 89(a), in determining whether
  a group of employees constitutes a unit appropriate for collective
  bargaining, the Board must have regard to the employer's
  classification of persons and positions, including the occupational
  groups or subgroups established by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unit co-extensive with occupational
  groups

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board must establish bargaining units that are
  co-extensive with the occupational groups or subgroups established by
  the employer, unless doing so would not permit satisfactory
  representation of the employees to be included in a particular
  bargaining unit and, for that reason, such a unit would not be
  appropriate for collective bargaining.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of questions of membership
in bargaining units

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** On application by the new
separate agency or the employee organization affected, the Board must
determine every question that arises as to whether any employee or class
of employees is included in a bargaining unit determined by the Board
under paragraph 84(1)(a) or 89(a) to constitute a unit appropriate for
collective bargaining, or is included in any other unit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer participation

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may not declare an employee organization to be a bargaining
  agent under paragraph 84(1)(b) or 89(a) if it is of the opinion that
  the new separate agency, or a person acting on behalf of the new
  separate agency, has participated or is participating in the formation
  or administration of the employee organization in a manner that
  impairs its fitness to represent the interests of the employees in the
  bargaining unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discrimination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board may not declare an employee organization to
  be a bargaining agent under paragraph 84(1)(b) or 89(a) if it
  discriminates against any employee on a prohibited ground of
  discrimination within the meaning of the [Canadian Human Rights
  Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

#### [Revocation of Certification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-405333 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When employee organization no longer
represents employees

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person claiming to represent a majority of the employees in a
  bargaining unit bound by a collective agreement or an arbitral award
  may apply to the Board for a declaration that the employee
  organization that is certified as the bargaining agent for the
  bargaining unit no longer represents a majority of the employees in
  the bargaining unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application may be made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application may be made only during the period in
  which an application for certification of an employee organization may
  be made under section 55 in respect of employees in the bargaining
  unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "94"
- 2014, c. 40, s. 11
- 2017, c. 12, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-94-20150616.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Taking of representation vote

**[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** After the application is
made, the Board may order that a representation vote be taken to
determine whether a majority of the employees in the bargaining unit no
longer wish to be represented by the employee organization that is the
bargaining agent for that bargaining unit. The provisions of subsection
65(2) apply in relation to the taking of the vote.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "95"
- 2014, c. 40, s. 12
- 2017, c. 12, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-95-20150616.html "Link to previous version of section 95"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of certification

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** If, after hearing the
application, the Board is satisfied that a majority of the employees in
the bargaining unit no longer wish to be represented by the employee
organization, it must revoke the certification of the employee
organization as the bargaining agent.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "96"
- 2014, c. 40, s. 12
- 2017, c. 12, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-96-20150616.html "Link to previous version of section 96"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification obtained by fraud

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** The Board must revoke the
certification of an employee organization if the Board is satisfied that
it was obtained by fraud.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer participation or discrimination

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** The Board must revoke the
certification of an employee organization as the bargaining agent for a
bargaining unit if the Board, on application by the employer or any
employee, determines that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer, or a person acting on behalf of the
  employer, has participated or is participating in the formation or
  administration of the employee organization in a manner that impairs
  its fitness to represent the interests of the employees in the
  bargaining unit; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization discriminates against any
  employee on a prohibited ground of discrimination within the meaning
  of the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandonment of certification

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** The Board must revoke the
certification of an employee organization if the employee organization
advises the Board that it wishes to give up or abandon its certification
or if the Board, on application by the employer or any employee,
determines that the employee organization has ceased to act as
bargaining agent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Council of employee organizations

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board must revoke the certification of a council of employee
  organizations that has been certified as a bargaining agent if the
  Board is satisfied, on application by the employer or an employee
  organization that forms or has formed part of the council, that the
  council no longer meets the condition for certification set out in
  paragraph 64(1)(c) for a council of employee organizations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional circumstances

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The circumstances set out in subsection (1) apply in
  addition to the circumstances in which a certification may be revoked
  under sections 94 to 99 and section 238.17.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "100"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-100-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 100"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of revocation

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Revocation of the certification of
  an employee organization certified as the bargaining agent for a
  bargaining unit has the following effects:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph 67(c), any collective
    agreement or arbitral award that is binding on the employees in the
    bargaining unit ceases to be in force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), any rights or privileges
    flowing from the certification are terminated; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph 67(e), any essential services
    agreement that is in force in respect of positions in the bargaining
    unit ceases to be in force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of rights of bargaining
  agent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the certification of an employee organization is
  revoked by the Board under section 96, any of sections 98 to 100 or
  section 238.17, the Board must, on application by the employee
  organization or any employee organization that is substituted in the
  place of a bargaining agent under paragraph 67(c), determine any
  question as to any right or duty of the employee organization or of
  the substituted employee organization.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "101"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 301
- 2017, c. 9, s. 14
- 2018, c. 24, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction

**[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** If a collective
agreement or arbitral award ceases to be in force as a result of the
revocation of an employee organization's certification as the bargaining
agent for a bargaining unit, the Board must, on application by or on
behalf of any employee in the bargaining unit, by order, direct the
manner in which any right of the employee is to be recognized and given
effect.

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Choice of Process for Dispute Resolution]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405376 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Choice of process

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  bargaining agent for a bargaining unit must notify the Board, in
  accordance with the regulations, of the process it has chosen ---
  either arbitration or conciliation --- to be the process for the
  resolution of disputes to which it may be a party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recording of process

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board must record the process chosen by the
  bargaining agent for the resolution of disputes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period during which process to apply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The process recorded by the Board applies to the
  bargaining unit for the resolution of all disputes from the day on
  which a notice to bargain collectively in respect of the bargaining
  unit is given after the process is chosen, and it applies until the
  process is changed in accordance with section 104.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "103"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 302
- 2018, c. 24, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-103-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 103"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of process

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  bargaining agent for a bargaining unit that wishes to change the
  process for the resolution of a dispute that is applicable to the
  bargaining unit may apply to the Board, in accordance with the
  regulations, to record the change.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recording of change

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receiving the application, the Board must record
  the change of process.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date and duration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A change in the process for the resolution of a
  dispute becomes effective on the day that a notice to bargain
  collectively is given after the change is recorded and remains in
  force until the process is changed in accordance with this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "104"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 302
- 2018, c. 24, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 104"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Collective Bargaining and Collective Agreements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405395 .Subheading}

#### [Negotiation of Collective Agreements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-405396 .Subheading}

##### [Notice to Bargain Collectively]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405397 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to bargain collectively

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After the Board has certified an
  employee organization as the bargaining agent for a bargaining unit
  and the process for the resolution of a dispute applicable to that
  bargaining unit has been recorded by the Board, the bargaining agent
  or the employer may, by notice in writing, require the other to
  commence bargaining collectively with a view to entering into,
  renewing or revising a collective agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When notice may be given

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice to bargain collectively may be given

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at any time, if no collective agreement or arbitral
    award is in force and no request for arbitration has been made by
    either of the parties in accordance with this Part; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if a collective agreement or arbitral award is in
    force, within the four months before it ceases to be in force.

- [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 24, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of notice to Board

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A party that has given a notice to bargain
  collectively to another party must send a copy of the notice to the
  Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "105"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 303
- 2018, c. 24, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-105-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 105"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Effect of Notice]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405410 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to bargain in good faith

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** After the notice to
bargain collectively is given, the bargaining agent and the employer
must, without delay, and in any case within 20 days after the notice is
given unless the parties otherwise agree,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} meet and commence, or cause authorized
  representatives on their behalf to meet and commence, to bargain
  collectively in good faith; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} make every reasonable effort to enter into a
  collective agreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to observe terms and conditions

**[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** Unless the parties
otherwise agree, and subject to section 132, after the notice to bargain
collectively is given, each term and condition of employment applicable
to the employees in the bargaining unit to which the notice relates that
may be included in a collective agreement, and that is in force on the
day the notice is given, is continued in force and must be observed by
the employer, the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit and the
employees in the bargaining unit until a collective agreement is entered
into in respect of that term or condition or

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the process for the resolution of a dispute is
  arbitration, an arbitral award is rendered; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the process for the resolution of a dispute is
  conciliation, a strike could be declared or authorized without
  contravening subsection 194(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "107"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 304
- 2018, c. 24, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-107-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 107"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Mediation]{.HTitleText4} {#h-405421 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of mediator

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson may at any time, if requested to do so or on his or her
  own initiative, appoint a mediator to confer with the parties to a
