  labour practice under paragraph (1)(a) or (b) by reason only that the
  employer or person expresses their point of view, so long as they do
  not use coercion, intimidation, threats, promises or undue influence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The employer or a person does not commit an unfair
  labour practice under any of paragraphs (1)(a) or (b) or (2)(a) to (c)
  by reason only of any act or thing done or omitted in relation to a
  person who occupies, or is proposed to occupy, a managerial or
  confidential position or to a person who is an
  [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
  [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) or who occupies a
  position held by such an officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "186"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-186-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 186"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unfair representation by bargaining agent

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** No employee
organization that is certified as the bargaining agent for a bargaining
unit, and none of its officers and representatives, shall act in a
manner that is arbitrary or discriminatory or that is in bad faith in
the representation of any employee in the bargaining unit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unfair labour practices --- employee
organizations

**[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** No employee
organization and no officer or representative of an employee
organization or other person acting on behalf of an employee
organization shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} except with the consent of the employer, attempt, at
  an employee's place of employment during the employee's working hours,
  to persuade the employee to become, to refrain from becoming, to
  continue to be or to cease to be a member of an employee organization;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} expel or suspend an employee from membership in the
  employee organization or deny an employee membership in the employee
  organization by applying its membership rules to the employee in a
  discriminatory manner;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} take disciplinary action against or impose any form
  of penalty on an employee by applying the employee organization's
  standards of discipline to that employee in a discriminatory manner;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} expel or suspend an employee from membership in the
  employee organization, or take disciplinary action against, or impose
  any form of penalty on, an employee by reason of that employee having
  exercised any right under this Part or Part 2 or 2.1 or having refused
  to perform an act that is contrary to this Part or Division 1 of Part
  2.1; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} discriminate against a person with respect to
  membership in an employee organization, or intimidate or coerce a
  person or impose a financial or other penalty on a person, because
  that person has

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} testified or otherwise participated or may testify
    or otherwise participate in a proceeding under this Part or Part 2
    or 2.1,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} made an application or filed a complaint under
    this Part or Division 1 of Part 2.1 or presented a grievance under
    Part 2 or Division 2 of Part 2.1, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} exercised any right under this Part or Part 2 or
    2.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "188"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unfair labour practices --- persons

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no person
  shall seek by intimidation or coercion to compel an employee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to become, refrain from becoming or cease to be,
    or, except as otherwise provided in a collective agreement, to
    continue to be, a member of an employee organization; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to refrain from exercising any other right under
    this Part or Part 2 or 2.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person does not commit an unfair labour practice
  referred to in subsection (1) by reason of any act or thing done or
  omitted in relation to a person who occupies, or who is proposed to
  occupy, a managerial or confidential position or to a person who is an
  [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
  [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) or who occupies a
  position held by such an officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "189"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 23
:::

[Previous
Version](section-189-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 189"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 13]{.HLabel2}[Complaints]{.HTitleText2} {#h-405987 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints

- **[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board must examine and inquire into any complaint made to it that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer has failed to comply with section 56
    (duty to observe terms and conditions);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer or a bargaining agent has failed to
    comply with section 106 (duty to bargain in good faith);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employer, a bargaining agent or an employee has
    failed to comply with section 107 (duty to observe terms and
    conditions);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the employer, a bargaining agent or a deputy head
    has failed to comply with subsection 110(3) (duty to bargain in good
    faith);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the employer or an employee organization has failed
    to comply with section 117 (duty to implement provisions of the
    collective agreement) or 157 (duty to implement provisions of the
    arbitral award);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the employer, a bargaining agent or an employee has
    failed to comply with section 132 (duty to observe terms and
    conditions); or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the employer, an employee organization or any
    person has committed an unfair labour practice within the meaning of
    section 185.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for making complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), a complaint under
  subsection (1) must be made to the Board not later than 90 days after
  the date on which the complainant knew, or in the Board's opinion
  ought to have known, of the action or circumstances giving rise to the
  complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on complaints against
  employee organizations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), no complaint may be made
  to the Board under subsection (1) on the ground that an employee
  organization or any person acting on behalf of one has failed to
  comply with paragraph 188(b) or (c) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complainant has presented a grievance or appeal
    in accordance with any procedure that has been established by the
    employee organization and to which the complainant has been given
    ready access;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has dealt with the grievance or appeal of the
      complainant in a manner unsatisfactory to the complainant, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has not, within six months after the date on
      which the complainant first presented their grievance or appeal
      under paragraph (a), dealt with the grievance or appeal; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is made to the Board not later than
    90 days after the first day on which the complainant could, in
    accordance with paragraphs (a) and (b), make the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Board may, on application to it by a complainant,
  determine a complaint in respect of an alleged failure by an employee
  organization to comply with paragraph 188(b) or (c) that has not been
  presented as a grievance or appeal to the employee organization, if
  the Board is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the action or circumstance giving rise to the
    complaint is such that the complaint should be dealt with without
    delay; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization has not given the
    complainant ready access to a grievance or appeal procedure.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "190"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 320
- 2018, c. 24, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-190-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 190"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty and power of the Board

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), on
  receipt of a complaint made under subsection 190(1), the Board may
  assist the parties to the complaint to settle the complaint. If it
  decides not to do so or if the complaint is not settled within a
  period that the Board considers to be reasonable in the circumstances,
  it must determine the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to determine complaint
  involving collective agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board may refuse to determine a complaint made
  under subsection 190(1) in respect of a matter that, in the Board's
  opinion, could be referred to adjudication under Part 2 or Division 2
  of Part 2.1 by the complainant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a complaint is made in writing under subsection
  190(1) in respect of an alleged failure by the employer or any person
  acting on behalf of the employer to comply with subsection 186(2), the
  written complaint is itself evidence that the failure actually
  occurred and, if any party to the complaint proceedings alleges that
  the failure did not occur, the burden of proving that it did not is on
  that party.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "191"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 191"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Board determines that a complaint referred to in subsection 190(1)
  is well founded, the Board may make any order that it considers
  necessary in the circumstances against the party complained of,
  including any of the following orders:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has failed to comply with section
    107 or 132, an order requiring the employer to pay to any employee
    compensation that is not more than the amount that, in the Board's
    opinion, is equivalent to the remuneration that would, but for that
    failure, have been paid by the employer to the employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has failed to comply with paragraph
    186(2)(a), an order requiring the employer to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} employ, continue to employ or permit to return to
      the duties of their employment any person whom the employer or any
      person acting on the employer's behalf has refused to employ or
      continue to employ, has suspended, transferred, laid off,
      discharged for the promotion of economy and efficiency in the
      Royal Canadian Mounted Police or otherwise discriminated against,
      or discharged contrary to that paragraph,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} pay to any person affected by that failure
      compensation in an amount that is not more than, in the Board's
      opinion, the remuneration that would, but for that failure, have
      been paid by the employer to that person, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} rescind any disciplinary action taken in
      respect of any person affected by that failure and pay
      compensation in an amount that is not more than, in the Board's
      opinion, any financial or other penalty imposed on the person by
      the employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has failed to comply with paragraph
    186(2)(c), an order requiring the employer to rescind any action
    taken in respect of any employee affected by the failure and pay
    compensation in an amount that is not more than, in the Board's
    opinion, any financial or other penalty imposed on the employee by
    the employer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if an employee organization has failed to comply
    with section 187, an order requiring the employee organization to
    take and carry on on behalf of any employee affected by the failure
    or to assist any such employee to take and carry on any proceeding
    that the Board considers that the employee organization ought to
    have taken and carried on on the employee's behalf or ought to have
    assisted the employee to take and carry on;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if an employee organization has failed to comply
    with paragraph 188(b) or (d), an order requiring the employee
    organization to reinstate or admit an employee as one of its
    members; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if an employee organization has failed to comply
    with paragraph 188(c), (d) or (e), an order requiring the employee
    organization to rescind any disciplinary action taken in respect of
    any employee affected by the failure and pay compensation in an
    amount that is not more than, in the Board's opinion, any financial
    or other penalty imposed on the employee by the employee
    organization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person acting on behalf of employer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the order is directed to a person who has acted or
  purported to act on behalf of the employer, the order must also be
  directed to the Secretary of the Treasury Board in the case of the
  core public administration and, in the case of a separate agency, to
  its deputy head.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person acting on behalf of employee
  organization

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the order is directed to a person who has acted or
  purported to act on behalf of an employee organization, the order must
  also be directed to the chief officer of that employee organization.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "192"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 321
- 2017, c. 9, s. 25(E)
- 2018, c. 24, s. 22
:::

Version](section-192-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 192"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 14]{.HLabel2}[Prohibitions and Enforcement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406040 .Subheading}

#### [Acts of Officers and Representatives of Employee Organizations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406041 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acts deemed to be those of employee
organization

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, an act or thing done or omitted by an officer or
representative of an employee organization within the scope of that
person's authority to act on the employee organization's behalf is
deemed to be an act or thing done or omitted by the employee
organization.

#### [Prohibitions Relating to Strikes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406044 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration or authorization of strike
prohibited

- **[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  employee organization shall declare or authorize a strike in respect
  of a bargaining unit, and no officer or representative of an employee
  organization shall counsel or procure the declaration or authorization
  of a strike in respect of a bargaining unit or the participation of
  employees in such a strike, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization has not been certified by
    the Board as the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a collective agreement applying to the bargaining
    unit is in force;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} no collective agreement is in force, and no notice
    to bargain collectively has been given, in respect of the bargaining
    unit;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} no collective agreement is in force, and a notice
    to bargain collectively has been given, in respect of the bargaining
    unit, and no request for conciliation has been made under section
    161;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the process for resolution of a dispute applicable
    to the bargaining unit is arbitration;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the process for resolution of a dispute applicable
    to the bargaining unit is conciliation and a notice to enter into an
    essential services agreement has been given under section 122 by the
    employer or the employee organization, as bargaining agent for the
    bargaining unit, and no essential services agreement is in force in
    respect of the bargaining unit;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the process for resolution of a dispute in respect
    of the bargaining unit is conciliation and a notice to amend an
    essential services agreement has been given under section 126 by the
    employer or the employee organization, as bargaining agent for the
    bargaining unit, and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the essential services agreement has not been
      amended as a result of that notice, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if an application referred to in subsection
      127(1) was made by the employer or the employee organization, the
      application has not been finally disposed of by the Board;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the process for resolution of a dispute in respect
    of the bargaining unit is conciliation and less than 30 clear days
    have elapsed since

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date an essential services agreement came
      into force in respect of the bargaining unit, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date an application referred to in
      subsection 123(1) made by the employer or the employee
      organization as bargaining agent for the bargaining unit was
      finally disposed of by the Board, if such an application was made;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the process for resolution of a dispute in respect
    of the bargaining unit is conciliation and the employer or the
    employee organization, as bargaining agent for the bargaining unit,
    has given a notice to amend an essential services agreement and less
    than 30 clear days have elapsed since

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of the coming into force of the
      amendment to the essential services agreement as a result of that
      notice, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date the application referred to in
      subsection 127(1) made by the employer or the employee
      organization was finally disposed of by the Board, if such an
      application was made;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an essential services agreement binding on the
    employee organization and the employer has been suspended by order
    under section 131;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the process for resolution of a dispute in respect
    of the bargaining unit is conciliation and a public interest
    commission has not been established to assist the employer and the
    employee organization, as bargaining agent for the bargaining unit,
    to enter into or revise a collective agreement, unless the employee
    organization has been notified under subsection 162(3) that a public
    interest commission will not be established;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization, as bargaining agent for
    the bargaining unit, has been notified under subsection 162(3) that
    a public interest commission will not be established to assist the
    employer and the employee organization to enter into or revise a
    collective agreement and less than seven clear days have elapsed
    since the date the notice was given under that subsection;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a public interest commission has been established
    to assist the employer and the employee organization, as bargaining
    agent for the bargaining unit, to enter into or revise a collective
    agreement and the Chairperson has not yet sent the commission's
    report or reconsidered report, as the case may be, to the parties,
    or, if it has been sent, less than seven clear days have elapsed
    since it was sent;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization, as bargaining agent for
    the bargaining unit, has agreed with the employer to be bound as
    described in section 181 in respect of all terms and conditions in
    dispute;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization, as bargaining agent for
    the bargaining unit, has agreed with the employer to refer, under
    subsection 182(1), all terms and conditions in dispute to final and
    binding determination;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} a vote has been held under subsection 183(1) and a
    majority of employees participating in the vote have accepted the
    employer's last offer;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization has failed to conduct a
    secret ballot vote in accordance with section 184; or

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization has conducted a secret
    ballot vote in accordance with section 184 and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it has not received the approval of a majority of
      the employees who voted, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if it has received the approval of a majority of
      the employees who voted, more than 60 clear days, or any longer
      period that may be agreed to in writing by the employee
      organization and the employer, have elapsed since the vote was
      held.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Essential services

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No employee organization shall declare or authorize a
  strike the effect of which is or would be to involve the participation
  of any employee who occupies a position that is necessary under an
  essential services agreement for the employer to provide essential
  services, and no officer or representative of an employee organization
  shall counsel or procure the participation of those employees in a
  strike.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "194"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 322
- 2018, c. 24, s. 23
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-194-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 194"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-employees

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** No person employed in
the public service shall participate in a strike if the person is not an
employee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participation prohibited

**[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall
participate in a strike if the employee

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not included in a bargaining unit for which a
  bargaining agent has been certified by the Board;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  a collective agreement is in force;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  no collective agreement is in force and for which no notice to bargain
  collectively has been given;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  no collective agreement is in force and for which a notice to bargain
  collectively has been given and in respect of which no request for
  conciliation has been made under section 161;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit for which the
  process for resolution of a dispute is arbitration;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit for which the
  process for resolution of a dispute is conciliation and in respect of
  which a notice to enter into an essential services agreement has been
  given under section 122 by the employer or the bargaining agent for
  the bargaining unit, and no essential services agreement is in force
  in respect of the bargaining unit;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit for which the
  process for resolution of a dispute is conciliation and in respect of
  which a notice to amend an essential services agreement has been given
  under section 126 by the employer or the bargaining agent for the
  bargaining unit, and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the essential services agreement has not been
    amended as a result of that notice, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if an application referred to in subsection 127(1)
    was made by the employer or the bargaining agent, the application
    has not been finally disposed of by the Board;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit for which the
  process for resolution of a dispute is conciliation and less than 30
  clear days have elapsed since

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date an essential services agreement came into
    force in respect of the bargaining unit, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date an application referred to in subsection
    123(1) made by the employer or the bargaining agent for the
    bargaining unit was finally disposed of by the Board, if such an
    application was made;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit for which the
  process for resolution of a dispute is conciliation and in respect of
  which the employer or the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit has
  given a notice to amend an essential services agreement and less than
  30 clear days have elapsed since

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of the coming into force of the amendment
    to the essential services agreement as a result of that notice, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date the application referred to in subsection
    127(1) made by the employer or the employee organization was finally
    disposed of by the Board, if such an application was made;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} occupies a position that is necessary under an
  essential services agreement for the employer to provide essential
  services;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  an essential services agreement binding on the bargaining agent for
  the bargaining unit and the employer has been suspended by order under
  section 131;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit for which the
  process for resolution of a dispute is conciliation and in respect of
  which a public interest commission has not been established to assist
  the employer and the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit to enter
  into or revise a collective agreement, unless the bargaining agent has
  been notified under subsection 162(3) that a public interest
  commission will not be established;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit has been notified under
  subsection 162(3) that a public interest commission will not be
  established to assist the employer and the bargaining agent to enter
  into or revise a collective agreement and less than seven clear days
  have elapsed from the date the notice was given under that subsection;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  a public interest commission has been established to assist the
  employer and the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit to enter
  into or revise a collective agreement, and the Chairperson has not yet
  sent the commission's report or reconsidered report, as the case may
  be, to the parties, or, if it has been sent, less than seven clear
  days have elapsed since it was sent;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit whose bargaining
  agent has agreed with the employer to be bound as described in section
  181 in respect of all terms and conditions in dispute;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit whose bargaining
  agent has agreed with the employer to refer, under subsection 182(1),
  all terms and conditions in dispute to final and binding
  determination;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit in respect of which
  a vote has been held under subsection 183(1) and a majority of
  employees participating in the vote have accepted the employer's last
  offer;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit whose bargaining
  agent has failed to conduct a secret ballot vote in accordance with
  section 184; or

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} is included in a bargaining unit whose bargaining
  agent has conducted a secret ballot vote in accordance with section
  184 and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the bargaining agent has not received the approval
    of a majority of the employees who voted, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the bargaining agent has received the approval
    of a majority of the employees who voted and the bargaining agent
    has declared or authorized the strike after 60 clear days have
    elapsed, or after any longer period that may be agreed to in writing
    by the bargaining agent and the employer has elapsed, since the day
    the vote was held.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "196"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 323
- 2018, c. 24, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 196"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to strike limited during period
between Parliaments

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  strike occurs or may occur during the period beginning on the date of
  a dissolution of Parliament and ending on the date fixed for the
  return of the writs at the next following general election and, in the
  opinion of the Governor in Council, the strike adversely affects or
  would adversely affect the national interest, the Governor in Council
  may during that period make an order deferring the strike during the
  period beginning on the day on which the order is made and ending on
  the twenty-first day following the date fixed for the return of the
  writs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling in Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must cause a report giving the reasons
  for the order to be tabled in each House of Parliament within the
  first 10 days on which that House is sitting after the order is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration or authorization of strike
  prohibited during deferred period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No employee organization shall declare or authorize,
  and no officer or representative of an employee organization shall
  counsel or procure the declaration or authorization of, a strike in
  respect of a bargaining unit in the period during which the strike is
  deferred by an order made under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participation prohibited during
  deferred period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall participate in a strike in the
  period during which the strike is deferred by an order made under
  subsection (1).

#### [Declarations and Orders Relating to Strikes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406120 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for declaration that conduct
is unlawful

- **[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the employer considers that an employee organization or any officer or
  representative of one has contravened subsection 194(1) or (2) or
  197(3), or that a person has contravened section 195, or that an
  employee has contravened section 196 or subsection 197(4), the
  employer may apply to the Board for a declaration that the conduct
  giving rise to the contravention is unlawful.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration that strike unlawful and
  strike prohibited

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After affording the employee organization, officer,
  representative, person or employee referred to in subsection (1) an
  opportunity to make representations on the application, the Board may
  declare that the conduct is unlawful and, if the employer so requests,
  may make an order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} requiring the employee organization to revoke the
    declaration or authorization of a strike and to give notice
    immediately of its revocation to the employees to whom it was
    directed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enjoining the officer or representative from
    counselling or procuring the declaration or authorization of a
    strike or the participation of employees in a strike;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} enjoining the employee from participating in the
    strike;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} requiring the employee to perform the duties of his
    or her employment; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} requiring any employee organization, of which any
    employee with respect to whom an order is made under paragraph (c)
    or (d) is a member, and any officer or representative of that
    employee organization, to immediately give notice of any order made
    under one of those paragraphs to the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and duration of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must be in terms that the Board considers necessary
    and sufficient to meet the circumstances of the case; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4), has effect for the time
    specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation or revocation of order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On application by the employer or employee
  organization or any employee or other person affected by an order made
  under subsection (2), notice of which has been given to the parties
  named in the order, the Board may, by supplementary order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} continue the order, with or without modification,
    for the period stated in the supplementary order; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} revoke the order.

#### [Prohibition Relating to Essential Services]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406139 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obstruction

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** No person shall impede
or prevent or attempt to impede or prevent an employee from entering or
leaving the employee's place of work if the employee occupies a position
that is necessary under an essential services agreement for the employer
to provide essential services.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "199"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 324
- 2018, c. 24, s. 25
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-199-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 199"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Prohibition Relating to Counselling]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406144 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counselling in respect of peace officer
duties

**[[199.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199.1 .sectionLabel}** No employee
organization and no officer or representative of an employee
organization shall counsel any RCMP member or reservist not to carry out
their duties as a peace officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 26
:::

#### [Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406149 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes subsection 186(1) or (2), section 188, subsection 189(1) or
section 195 or 199 is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
conviction to a fine of not more than \$1,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employees

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** Every employee who
contravenes section 196 or subsection 197(4) is guilty of an offence and
liable on summary conviction to a fine of not more than \$1,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee organizations

- **[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee organization that
  contravenes, and every officer or representative of one who
  contravenes, section 187 or 188 is guilty of an offence and liable on
  summary conviction to a fine of not more than \$1,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee organizations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee organization that contravenes
  subsection 194(1) or (2) or 197(3) is guilty of an offence and liable
  on summary conviction to a fine not more than \$1,000 for each day
  that any strike declared or authorized by it in contravention of that
  subsection is in effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee organization

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employee organization that contravenes section
  199.1 is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a
  fine of not more than \$10,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prosecution of employee organization

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A prosecution for an offence under any of
  subsections (1) to (3) may be brought against an employee organization
  and in the name of that organization and, for the purposes of the
  prosecution, the employee organization is deemed to be a person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "202"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 202"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers and representatives of employee
organizations

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** Every officer or
representative of an employee organization who contravenes subsection
194(1) or section 199.1 is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
conviction to a fine of not more than \$10,000.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "203"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 203"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** If the employer
contravenes subsection 186(1) or (2), the employer is guilty of an
offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine of not more than
\$10,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent to prosecution

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** A prosecution for an
offence under this Division may be instituted only with the consent of
the Board.

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Grievances]{.HTitleText1} {#h-406173 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406174 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this Part.

  [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person employed in the
      public service, other than

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person appointed by the Governor in Council
        under an Act of Parliament to a statutory position described in
        that Act;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person locally engaged outside Canada;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person not ordinarily required to work more
        than one third of the normal period for persons doing similar
        work;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who is an
        [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1)
        of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10);

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person employed on a casual basis;

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person employed on a term basis, unless the
        term of employment is for a period of three months or more or
        the person has been so employed for a period of three months or
        more;

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined
        in subsection 2(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
        Act](/eng/acts/R-10) who occupies a managerial or confidential
        position; or

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a person who is employed under a program
        designated by the employer as a student employment
        program. ([fonctionnaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[group grievance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[group grievance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a grievance
      presented in accordance with section 215. ([grief
      collectif]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[individual grievance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[individual grievance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a grievance
      presented in accordance with section 208 or 238.24. ([grief
      individuel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[policy grievance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[policy grievance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a grievance
      presented in accordance with section 220. ([grief de
      principe]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former employees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every reference in this Part to an
  [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a former employee for the
  purposes of any provisions of this Part respecting grievances with
  respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any disciplinary action resulting in suspension, or
    any termination of employment, under paragraph 12(1)(c), (d) or (e)
    of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a separate agency, any disciplinary
    action resulting in suspension, or any termination of employment,
    under paragraph 12(2)(c) or (d) of the [Financial Administration
    Act](/eng/acts/F-11) or under any provision of any Act of
    Parliament, or any regulation, order or other instrument made under
    the authority of an Act of Parliament, respecting the powers or
    functions of the separate agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "206"
- 2014, c. 20, s. 481
- 2017, c. 9, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Conflict Management]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406196 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Informal conflict management system

**[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** Subject to any policies
established by the employer or any directives issued by it, every deputy
head in the core public administration must, in consultation with
bargaining agents representing employees in the portion of the core
public administration for which he or she is deputy head, establish an
informal conflict management system and inform the employees in that
portion of its availability.

### [Individual Grievances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406199 .Subheading}

#### [Presentation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406200 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of employee

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (7),
  an employee is entitled to present an individual grievance if he or
  she feels aggrieved

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by the interpretation or application, in respect of
    the employee, of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a provision of a statute or regulation, or of a
      direction or other instrument made or issued by the employer, that
      deals with terms and conditions of employment, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a provision of a collective agreement or an
      arbitral award; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as a result of any occurrence or matter affecting
    his or her terms and conditions of employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee may not present an individual grievance
  in respect of which an administrative procedure for redress is
  provided under any Act of Parliament, other than the [Canadian Human
  Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), an employee may not present
  an individual grievance in respect of the right to equal pay for work
  of equal value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employee may not present an individual grievance
  relating to the interpretation or application, in respect of the
  employee, of a provision of a collective agreement or an arbitral
  award unless the employee has the approval of and is represented by
  the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit to which the collective
  agreement or arbitral award applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employee who, in respect of any matter, avails
  himself or herself of a complaint procedure established by a policy of
  the employer may not present an individual grievance in respect of
  that matter if the policy expressly provides that an employee who
  avails himself or herself of the complaint procedure is precluded from
  presenting an individual grievance under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An employee may not present an individual grievance
  relating to any action taken under any instruction, direction or
  regulation given or made by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be conclusive proof

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (6), an order made by
  the Governor in Council is conclusive proof of the matters stated in
  the order in relation to the giving or making of an instruction, a
  direction or a regulation by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

#### [Reference to Adjudication]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406220 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to adjudication

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee who is not a [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in
  subsection 2(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
  Act](/eng/acts/R-10) may refer to adjudication an individual grievance
  that has been presented up to and including the final level in the
  grievance process and that has not been dealt with to the employee's
  satisfaction if the grievance is related to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the interpretation or application in respect of the
    employee of a provision of a collective agreement or an arbitral
    award;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a disciplinary action resulting in termination,
    demotion, suspension or financial penalty;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee in the core public
    administration,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} demotion or termination under paragraph 12(1)(d)
      of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11) for
      unsatisfactory performance or under paragraph 12(1)(e) of that Act
      for any other reason that does not relate to a breach of
      discipline or misconduct, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} deployment under the [Public Service Employment
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01) without the employee's consent where
      consent is required; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee of a separate agency
    designated under subsection (3), demotion or termination for any
    reason that does not relate to a breach of discipline or misconduct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of paragraph (1)(a)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before referring an individual grievance related to
  matters referred to in paragraph (1)(a), the employee must obtain the
  approval of his or her bargaining agent to represent him or her in the
  adjudication proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, designate any
  separate agency for the purposes of paragraph (1)(d).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "209"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 30
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to adjudication

**[[209.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.1 .sectionLabel}** In addition to his
or her rights under section 209, an employee, other than an employee who
occupies a managerial or confidential position or who is not otherwise
represented by a bargaining agent, may refer to adjudication an
individual grievance that has been presented up to and including the
final level in the grievance process and that has not been dealt with to
the employee's satisfaction if the employee has suffered physical or
psychological harm, property damage or economic loss as the result of
--- or has otherwise been adversely affected by --- the contravention of
a provision of regulations made under subsection 117(1) of the
[Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6), and the grievance is related
to that contravention.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 184](#1154797-1191857){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154797-1191857.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Canadian Human Rights
Commission

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  an individual grievance has been referred to adjudication and a party
  to the grievance raises an issue involving the interpretation or
  application of the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6), that
  party must, in accordance with the regulations, give notice of the
  issue to the Canadian Human Rights Commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Commission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Human Rights Commission has standing in
  adjudication proceedings for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding an issue referred to in subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Accessibility Commissioner

- **[[210.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When an individual grievance has
  been referred to adjudication and a party to the grievance raises an
  issue involving the contravention of a provision of regulations made
  under subsection 117(1) of the [Accessible Canada
  Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6), that party must, in accordance with the
  regulations, give notice of the issue to the [[Accessibility
  Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 2 of that
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Accessibility Commissioner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Accessibility Commissioner has standing in
  adjudication proceedings for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding an issue referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 185](#1154799-1191864){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154799-1191864.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in section 209
or 209.1 is to be construed or applied as permitting the referral to
adjudication of an individual grievance with respect to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any termination of employment under the [Public
  Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01); or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any deployment under the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01), other than the deployment of the employee who
  presented the grievance.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "211"
- [2019, c. 10, s. 186](#1154801-1191868){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154801-1191868.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Representation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406245 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to be represented by employee
organization

**[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** An employee who is not
included in a bargaining unit for which an employee organization has
been certified as bargaining agent may seek the assistance of, and, if
the employee chooses, may be represented by, any employee organization
in the presentation or reference to adjudication of an individual
grievance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to be represented by employee
organization

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** No employee who is
included in a bargaining unit for which an employee organization has
been certified as bargaining agent may be represented by any other
employee organization in the presentation or reference to adjudication
of an individual grievance.

#### [Binding Effect]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406250 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision final and binding

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** If an individual
grievance has been presented up to and including the final level in the
grievance process and it is not one that under section 209, 209.1 or
238.25 may be referred to adjudication, the decision on the grievance
taken at the final level in the grievance process is final and binding
for all purposes of this Act and no further action under this Act may be
taken on it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "214"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 31
- [2019, c. 10, s. 187](#1154803-1191871){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154803-1191871.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Group Grievances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406255 .Subheading}

#### [Presentation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406256 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of bargaining agent

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  bargaining agent for a bargaining unit may present to the employer a
  group grievance on behalf of employees in the bargaining unit who feel
  aggrieved by the interpretation or application, common in respect of
  those employees, of a provision of a collective agreement or an
  arbitral award.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In order to present the grievance, the bargaining
  agent must first obtain the consent of each of the employees concerned
  in the form provided for by the regulations. The consent of an
  employee is valid only in respect of the particular group grievance
  for which it is obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single portion

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The group grievance must relate to employees in a
  single portion of the federal public administration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A bargaining agent may not present a group grievance
  in respect of which an administrative procedure for redress is
  provided under any Act of Parliament, other than the [Canadian Human
  Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (4), a bargaining agent may not
  present a group grievance in respect of the right to equal pay for
  work of equal value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If an employee has, in respect of any matter, availed
  himself or herself of a complaint procedure established by a policy of
  the employer, the bargaining agent may not include that employee as
  one on whose behalf it presents a group grievance in respect of that
  matter if the policy expressly provides that an employee who avails
  himself or herself of the complaint procedure is precluded from
  participating in a group grievance under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A bargaining agent may not present a group grievance
  relating to any action taken under any instruction, direction or
  regulation given or made by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be conclusive proof

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (7), an order made by
  the Governor in Council is conclusive proof of the matters stated in
  the order in relation to the giving or making of an instruction, a
  direction or a regulation by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

#### [Reference to Adjudication]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406274 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to adjudication

**[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** The bargaining agent
may refer to adjudication any group grievance that has been presented up
to and including the final level in the grievance process and that has
not been dealt with to its satisfaction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Canadian Human Rights
Commission

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a group grievance has been referred to adjudication and a party to the
  grievance raises an issue involving the interpretation or application
  of the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6), that party must, in
  accordance with the regulations, give notice of the issue to the
  Canadian Human Rights Commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Commission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Human Rights Commission has standing in
  adjudication proceedings for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding an issue referred to in subsection (1).

#### [Withdrawal from Group Grievance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406282 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Accessibility Commissioner

- **[[217.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a group grievance has been
  referred to adjudication and a party to the grievance raises an issue
  involving the contravention of a provision of regulations made under
  subsection 117(1) of the [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6),
  that party must, in accordance with the regulations, give notice of
  the issue to the [[Accessibility Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
  as defined in section 2 of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Accessibility Commissioner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Accessibility Commissioner has standing in
  adjudication proceedings for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding an issue referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 188](#1154807-1191878){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154807-1191878.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of employee to withdraw

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** An employee in respect
of whom a group grievance has been presented may, at any time before a
final decision is made in respect of the grievance, notify the
bargaining agent that the employee no longer wishes to be involved in
the group grievance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of notice

**[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** After receiving the
notice, the bargaining agent may not pursue the grievance in respect of
the employee.

### [Policy Grievances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406287 .Subheading}

#### [Presentation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406288 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of employer and bargaining agent

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the employer and a bargaining agent are bound by an arbitral award or
  have entered into a collective agreement, either of them may present a
  policy grievance to the other in respect of the interpretation or
  application of the collective agreement or arbitral award as it
  relates to either of them or to the bargaining unit generally.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Neither the employer nor a bargaining agent may
  present a policy grievance in respect of which an administrative
  procedure for redress is provided under any other Act of Parliament,
  other than the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), neither the employer nor a
  bargaining agent may present a policy grievance in respect of the
  right to equal pay for work of equal value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A bargaining agent may not present a policy grievance
  relating to any action taken under any instruction, direction or
  regulation given or made by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be conclusive proof

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (4), an order made by
  the Governor in Council is conclusive proof of the matters stated in
  the order in relation to the giving or making of an instruction, a
  direction or a regulation by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

#### [Reference to Adjudication]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406300 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to adjudication

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** A party that presents a
policy grievance may refer it to adjudication.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Canadian Human Rights
Commission

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a policy grievance has been referred to adjudication and a party to
  the grievance raises an issue involving the interpretation or
  application of the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6), that
  party must, in accordance with the regulations, give notice of the
  issue to the Canadian Human Rights Commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Commission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Human Rights Commission has standing in
  adjudication proceedings for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding an issue referred to in subsection (1).

### [Adjudication]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406308 .Subheading}

#### [Notice to Board]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406309 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Accessibility Commissioner

- **[[222.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a policy grievance has been
  referred to adjudication and a party to the grievance raises an issue
  involving the contravention of a provision of regulations made under
  subsection 117(1) of the [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6),
  that party must, in accordance with the regulations, give notice of
  the issue to the [[Accessibility Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
  as defined in section 2 of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Accessibility Commissioner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Accessibility Commissioner has standing in
  adjudication proceedings for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding an issue referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 189](#1154809-1191885){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154809-1191885.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

- **[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party who refers a grievance to adjudication must, in accordance with
  the regulations, give notice of the reference to the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action to be taken by Chairperson

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the party specifies in the notice that an
  adjudicator is named in an applicable collective agreement, that an
  adjudicator has otherwise been selected by the parties or, if no
  adjudicator is so named or has been selected, that the party requests
  the establishment of a board of adjudication, then the Chairperson
  must, on receipt of the notice by the Board,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the grievance is one arising out of a collective
    agreement and an adjudicator is named in the agreement, refer the
    matter to the adjudicator;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the parties have selected an adjudicator, refer
    the matter to the adjudicator; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a board of adjudication has been requested and
    the other party has not objected in the time provided for in the
    regulations, establish the board and refer the matter to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board seized of grievance

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If the notice does not specify any of the things
  described in subsection (2), or if a board of adjudication has been
  requested and the other party has objected in the time provided for in
  the regulations, the Board is seized of the grievance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conference

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may, at any time after receipt of the
  notice, direct the parties to attend a conference in order to attempt
  to settle or simplify the issues in dispute.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "223"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 374
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-223-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 223"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Board of Adjudication]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406324 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constitution

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  board of adjudication consists of one member of the Board designated
  by the Chairperson, who is the chairperson of the board of
  adjudication, and two other persons, each of whom is nominated by one
  of the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ineligibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person is not eligible to be a member of a board of
  adjudication if the person has any direct interest in or connection
  with the grievance referred to the board of adjudication, its handling
  or its disposition.

#### [Jurisdiction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406330 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with procedures

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** No grievance may be
referred to adjudication until the grievance has been presented at all
required levels in accordance with the applicable grievance process.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "225"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 375
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-225-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 225"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Powers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406335 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of adjudicator

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  adjudicator may, in relation to any matter referred to adjudication,
  exercise any of the powers set out in paragraph 16(d) of this Act and
  sections 20 to 23 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and
  Employment Board Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of adjudicator and Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An adjudicator or the Board may, in relation to any
  matter referred to adjudication,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} interpret and apply the [Canadian Human Rights
    Act](/eng/acts/H-6) and any other Act of Parliament relating to
    employment matters, other than the provisions of the [Canadian Human
    Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6) that are related to the right to equal
    pay for work of equal value, whether or not there is a conflict
    between the Act being interpreted and applied and the collective
    agreement, if any;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} interpret and apply the [Accessible Canada
    Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} give relief in accordance with paragraph 53(2)(e)
    or subsection 53(3) of the [Canadian Human Rights
    Act](/eng/acts/H-6);

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} give relief in accordance with section 102 of the
    [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} award interest in the case of grievances involving
    termination, demotion, suspension or financial penalty at a rate and
    for a period that the adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be,
    considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "226"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 376
- 2017, c. 9, s. 56
- [2019, c. 10, s. 190](#1154811-1191889){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154811-1191889.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-226-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 226"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2013, c.
40, s. 376\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 227"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Decision]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406347 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing of grievance

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  grievance is referred to adjudication, the adjudicator or the Board,
  as the case may be, must give both parties to the grievance an
  opportunity to be heard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision on grievance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After considering the grievance, the adjudicator or
  the Board, as the case may be, must render a decision, make the order
  that the adjudicator or the Board consider appropriate in the
  circumstances, and then send a copy of the order  ---  and, if there
  are written reasons for the decision, a copy of the reasons  ---  to
  each party, to the representative of each party and to the bargaining
  agent, if any, for the bargaining unit to which the employee whose
  grievance it is belongs. The adjudicator must also deposit a copy of
  the order and, if there are written reasons for the decision, a copy
  of the reasons, with the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of board of adjudication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a board of adjudication, a decision of
  a majority of the members on a grievance is deemed to be a decision of
  the board in respect of the grievance, and the decision must be signed
  by the chairperson of the board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision where majority cannot agree

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a majority of members of the board of adjudication
  cannot agree on the making of a decision, the decision of the
  chairperson of the board is deemed to be the decision of the board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "228"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 378
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-228-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 228"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision requiring amendment

**[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** An adjudicator's or the
Board's decision may not have the effect of requiring the amendment of a
collective agreement or an arbitral award.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "229"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 379
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-229-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 229"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of reasonableness of
opinion

**[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** In the case of an
employee in the core public administration or an employee of a separate
agency designated under subsection 209(3), in making a decision in
respect of an employee's individual grievance relating to a termination
of employment or demotion for unsatisfactory performance, an adjudicator
or the Board, as the case may be, must determine the termination or
demotion to have been for cause if the opinion of the deputy head that
the employee's performance was unsatisfactory is determined by the
adjudicator or the Board to have been reasonable.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "230"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 379
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-230-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 230"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of consent requirement

**[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** An adjudicator or the
Board, when seized of a grievance referred to in subparagraph
209(1)(c)(ii), may determine any question relating to whether

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} consent to being deployed was a condition of the
  employee's employment; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee harassed another person in the course of
  the employee's employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "231"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 380
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-231-20051231.html "Link to previous version of section 231"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision in respect of certain policy
grievances

**[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** If a policy grievance
relates to a matter that was or could have been the subject of an
individual grievance or a group grievance, an adjudicator's or the
Board's decision in respect of the policy grievance is limited to one or
more of the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} declaring the correct interpretation, application or
  administration of a collective agreement or an arbitral award;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} declaring that the collective agreement or arbitral
  award has been contravened; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} requiring the employer or bargaining agent, as the
  case may be, to interpret, apply or administer the collective
  agreement or arbitral award in a specified manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "232"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 381
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-232-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 232"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review by court

**[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** Subsections 34(1) and
(3) of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and Employment Board
Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35) apply, with any necessary modifications, to an
adjudicator's orders and decisions.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "233"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 382
- 2017, c. 9, s. 56
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-233-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 233"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of order in Federal Court

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board must, on the request in writing of any person who was a party to
  the proceedings that resulted in an order of an adjudicator or the
  Board, as the case may be, file a certified copy of the order,
  exclusive of the reasons for it, in the Federal Court, unless, in the
  opinion of the Board,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is no indication, or likelihood, of failure
    to comply with the order; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is another good reason why the filing of the
    order in the Federal Court would serve no useful purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 35 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour
  Relations and Employment Board Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35) does not apply
  to an order of the Board referred to in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of filing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order of an adjudicator or the Board becomes an
  order of the Federal Court when a certified copy of it is filed in
  that court, and it may subsequently be enforced as such.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "234"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 382
- 2017, c. 9, s. 56
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-234-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 234"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Expenses of Adjudication]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406395 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggrieved employee not represented by
agent

- **[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an aggrieved employee is not represented in the adjudication by a
  bargaining agent, the costs of the adjudication are to be borne by the
  Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggrieved employee represented by agent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an aggrieved employee is represented in the
  adjudication by a bargaining agent, the bargaining agent is liable to
  pay and must remit to the Board any part of the costs of the
  adjudication that may be determined by the Chairperson with the
  Board's approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any amount that by subsection (2) is payable to the
  Board by a bargaining agent may be recovered as a debt due to Her
  Majesty in right of Canada. The bargaining agent is deemed to be a
  person for the purposes of this subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "235"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 383
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-235-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 235"){.PITLink}
:::

### [No Right of Action]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406405 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disputes relating to employment

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  right of an employee to seek redress by way of grievance for any
  dispute relating to his or her terms or conditions of employment is in
  lieu of any right of action that the employee may have in relation to
  any act or omission giving rise to the dispute.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies whether or not the employee
  avails himself or herself of the right to present a grievance in any
  particular case and whether or not the grievance could be referred to
  adjudication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of an
  employee of a separate agency that has not been designated under
  subsection 209(3) if the dispute relates to his or her termination of
  employment for any reason that does not relate to a breach of
  discipline or misconduct.

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406413 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may make regulations respecting the processes for dealing with
  grievances, including regulations concerning

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner and form of presenting a grievance and,
    in the case of group grievances, the form of the consent of the
    employees concerned;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum number of levels in each grievance
    process;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which employees are to be advised of
    the names of the persons whose decision on a grievance constitutes a
    level in the grievance process, including the final level;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the time within which a grievance may be presented
    at any level in a grievance process;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which any level below the
    final level in a grievance process may be eliminated;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and the time within which a
    grievance may be referred to adjudication after it has been
    presented up to and including the final level in the grievance
    process;

  - [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} the manner of giving notice of an issue to the
    Accessibility Commissioner under this Part;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of rules of procedure for the
    hearing of a grievance;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the specification of the time within which and the
    persons to whom notices and other documents must be sent or given
    under this Part or Division 2 of Part 2.1, and when the notices are
    deemed to have been sent, given or received; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the manner of giving notice of an issue to the
    Canadian Human Rights Commission under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) respecting
  individual, group or policy grievances do not apply in respect of
  employees included in a bargaining unit for which a bargaining agent
  has been certified by the Board to the extent that the regulations are
  inconsistent with any provisions contained in a collective agreement
  entered into by the bargaining agent and the employer applicable to
  those employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "237"
- 2017, c. 9, s. 32
- [2019, c. 10, s. 191](#1154816-1191891){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154816-1191891.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-237-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 237"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** The Board may make
regulations respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner of giving notice under subsection 223(1)
  and the time for making objections under paragraph 223(2)(c); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and the time within which boards
  of adjudication are to be established.

## [PART 2.1]{.HLabel1}[Provisions Unique to the Royal Canadian Mounted Police]{.HTitleText1} {#h-406434 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406435 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[RCMP
Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[238.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this Part, [[RCMP
  Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Commissioner of the Royal
  Canadian Mounted Police.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[RCMP
  member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In Division 2 of this Part, [[RCMP
  member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned by the
  definition [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1) of the
  [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10).
