::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency with Part 1 or 2

- **[[238.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In the event of an inconsistency
  between a provision of this Part and a provision of Part 1 or 2, the
  provision of this Part prevails to the extent of the inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency --- clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Without limiting the generality of subsection (1),
  section 58, subsections 208(1) and 209(1) and (2) and section 209.1
  are inconsistent with this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of Part 1, in so far as they are
    applicable, apply to employees who are RCMP members or reservists
    unless there is an indication to the contrary; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of Part 2, in so far as they are
    applicable, apply to employees who are [[RCMP
    members]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 238.01(2),
    or reservists unless there is an indication to the contrary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
- [2019, c. 10, s. 192](#1154818-1191894){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154818-1191894.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238.02-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 238.02"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's right preserved

**[[238.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.03 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Act is to be construed as affecting the right or authority of the
Treasury Board to determine categories of
[[members]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
[Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties preserved

**[[238.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.04 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Act is to be construed as affecting any duties of
[[members]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
[Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) who are peace
officers, or any duties of reservists who are peace officers, including
duties imposed under that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

### [Unique Role as Police Organization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406462 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board's additional duty

**[[238.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.05 .sectionLabel}** In administering
this Act and in exercising the powers and performing the duties and
functions that are conferred or imposed on it by this Act, or as are
incidental to the attainment of the objects of this Act, including the
making of orders requiring compliance with this Act, with regulations
made under it or with decisions made in respect of a matter coming
before the Board, the Board must, in matters concerning RCMP members and
reservists, take into account the unique role of the Royal Canadian
Mounted Police as a police organization in protecting public safety and
national security and its need to deploy its members and reservists as
it sees fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Labour Relations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406467 .Subheading}

#### [Adjournment of Proceedings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406468 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prejudice to ongoing investigations or
proceedings

- **[[238.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.06
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Board must, on the request of
  the RCMP Commissioner or the employer, adjourn all proceedings in
  relation to any matter before the Board under this Division or Part 1
  that concerns a person who is or was an RCMP member or a reservist if
  the Board is satisfied that to continue those proceedings would
  prejudice an ongoing criminal investigation or an ongoing criminal or
  civil proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum period of adjournment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No adjournment may be for a period of more than 90
  days. However, the RCMP Commissioner or the employer may request more
  than one adjournment in relation to a matter.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Law Enforcement, Public Safety and National Security]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406476 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-disclosure

- **[[238.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The RCMP Commissioner may, in
  connection with any matter or proceeding before the Board under this
  Division or Part 1 that concerns a person who is or was an RCMP member
  or a reservist, object to the disclosure to the Board or to a party of
  any information whose disclosure would, in that Commissioner's
  opinion, be injurious to law enforcement, public safety or national
  security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of objection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the RCMP Commissioner objects to the disclosure of
  information under subsection (1), he or she must, as soon as feasible,
  give written notice of the objection and the reasons for it to the
  Board and to the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other provisions do not apply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The RCMP Commissioner may object to the disclosure of
  information under subsection (1) despite any provision of this Act or
  the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and Employment Board
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35) that would otherwise permit the Board to
  require that the information be disclosed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former judge

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the RCMP Commissioner objects to the disclosure of
  information under subsection (1), the Minister must, at the written
  request of the Board or a party, appoint a former judge of a superior
  court of a province or the Federal Court to review the information and
  to make an order under section 238.08.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath of secrecy

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In order to be appointed, the former judge must
  obtain a security clearance from the Government of Canada and take the
  oath of secrecy prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of appointment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must provide notice to the Board, to the
  parties and to the RCMP Commissioner when a former judge has been
  appointed in accordance with subsection (4). The Board, the parties
  and the RCMP Commissioner may make their representations to the former
  judge only within 30 days after the day on which the notice is sent or
  any longer period, not more than 60 days after the day on which the
  notice is sent, that the former judge may permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former judge to have access

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The former judge is to have access to the information
  about which an objection is made under subsection (1) for the purposes
  of the review.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order regarding disclosure

- **[[238.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.08
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the former judge concludes
  that the disclosure of the information about which an objection is
  made under subsection 238.07(1) would be injurious to law enforcement,
  public safety or national security, the former judge must order that
  the RCMP Commissioner's objection under section 238.07 to the
  disclosure of that information be dismissed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the former judge concludes that the disclosure of
  all or part of that information would be injurious to law enforcement,
  public safety or national security but that the public interest in
  disclosure outweighs in importance the public interest in
  non-disclosure, the former judge, after considering the public
  interest in disclosure, must order the RCMP Commissioner to disclose
  to the Board or to the party, as the case may be, all or that part of
  that information, a summary of that information or a written admission
  of facts relating to that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No disclosure required

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the former judge does not make an order under
  subsection (1) or order disclosure under subsection (2), they must
  make an order upholding the RCMP Commissioner's objection to the
  disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The order of the former judge must be made within 30
  days after the day on which the period referred to in subsection
  238.07(6) expires or within any longer period, not more than 60 days
  after the day on which the period referred to in that subsection
  expires, that the Minister permits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The former judge must not include in their order any
  information that reveals information that they conclude must not be
  disclosed, or any information from which that information may be
  inferred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order final

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The former judge's order is final and binding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The RCMP Commissioner's objection is not subject to
  review except to the extent and in the manner provided under this
  section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection

- **[[238.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.09
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No criminal, civil or administrative
  action or proceeding lies against a former judge, or any person acting
  on their behalf or under their direction, for anything done, reported
  or said in good faith in the exercise or purported exercise of any
  power, or the performance or purported performance of any duty or
  function, of the former judge under sections 238.07 and 238.08.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No summons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The former judge, or any person acting on their
  behalf or under their direction, is not, in respect of any matter
  coming to the knowledge of the former judge or that person as a result
  of exercising a power or performing a duty or function of the former
  judge, a competent or compellable witness in any proceeding other than
  a prosecution for an offence under this Act, the [Foreign Interference
  and Security of Information Act](/eng/acts/O-5) or section 132 or 136
  of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
- [2024, c. 16, s. 57](#1460885-1486945){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1460885-1486945.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238.09-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 238.09"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of information

**[[238.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.1 .sectionLabel}** Information about
which an objection is made under subsection 238.07(1) that is disclosed
by the RCMP Commissioner to the Board or to a party in connection with
the matter or proceeding that gave rise to the objection may be used
only in connection with that matter or proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Memorandum of understanding

**[[238.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.11 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson
and the RCMP Commissioner may enter into a memorandum of understanding
setting out principles and procedures respecting the disclosure of
information referred to in subsection 238.07(1) and the protection of
that information if it is disclosed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information --- other legal
proceedings

- **[[238.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the RCMP Commissioner discloses
  information about which an objection is made under subsection
  238.07(1) to the Board or to a party in connection with the matter or
  proceeding that gave rise to the objection, the Board or that party is
  not to be required, in connection with any other criminal, civil or
  administrative action or proceeding, to give or produce evidence
  relating to that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided by any other Act of Parliament
  that expressly refers to it, this section applies despite any other
  Act of Parliament, other than the [Access to Information
  Act](/eng/acts/A-1) and the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Section prevails

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section applies despite subsection 13(1) of the
  [Auditor General Act](/eng/acts/A-17) and subsection 79.3(1) of the
  [Parliament of Canada Act](/eng/acts/P-1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Certification of Bargaining Agents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406535 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to apply

- **[[238.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 55, an employee
  organization within the meaning of paragraph (b) of the definition
  [[employee organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1)
  that seeks to be certified as the bargaining agent for the group that
  consists exclusively of all the employees who are RCMP members and all
  the employees who are reservists may apply to the Board, in accordance
  with the regulations, for certification as bargaining agent for that
  group. The Board must notify the employer of the application without
  delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for certification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board may certify an employee organization
  referred to in subsection (1) as the bargaining agent for the group
  only if it determines that the employee organization --- and, in the
  case of a council of employee organizations, each employee
  organization forming the council --- meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it has as its primary mandate the representation of
    employees who are RCMP members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is not affiliated with a bargaining agent or
    other association that does not have as its primary mandate the
    representation of police officers; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it is not certified as bargaining agent for any
    other group of employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection (2), participation
    by an employee organization in the National Joint Council is not
    considered to be an affiliation with a bargaining agent or other
    association that does not have as its primary mandate the
    representation of police officers; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no application is permitted to be made under
    section 54 in respect of employees who are RCMP members or
    reservists.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of unit

**[[238.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.14 .sectionLabel}** If an application
for certification is made under subsection 238.13(1), the Board must
determine that the group that consists exclusively of all the employees
who are RCMP members and all the employees who are reservists
constitutes the single, national bargaining unit that is appropriate for
collective bargaining.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

**[[238.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.15 .sectionLabel}** An employee
organization that is certified as the bargaining agent for the
bargaining unit determined under section 238.14 is not entitled to seek
to be certified as bargaining agent for any other group of employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

**[[238.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.16 .sectionLabel}** Despite section
58, the Board does not have jurisdiction to determine that an employee
who is not an RCMP member or a reservist is included in the bargaining
unit determined under section 238.14.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Revocation of Certification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406562 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandate or affiliation

- **[[238.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Board must revoke the
  certification of an employee organization as the bargaining agent for
  the bargaining unit determined under section 238.14 if the Board, on
  application by the employer or any employee in that bargaining unit,
  determines that the employee organization --- or, in the case of a
  council of employee organizations, any employee organization forming
  part of the council --- no longer has as its primary mandate the
  representation of employees who are RCMP members or that it is
  affiliated with a bargaining agent or other association that does not
  have as its primary mandate the representation of police officers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection (1), participation
    by an employee organization in the National Joint Council is not
    considered to be an affiliation with a bargaining agent or other
    association that does not have as its primary mandate the
    representation of police officers; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances set out in subsection (1) apply
    in addition to the circumstances in which a certification may be
    revoked under sections 94 to 100.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Process for Dispute Resolution]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406572 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration

**[[238.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.18 .sectionLabel}** Sections 103 and
104 do not apply to disputes between the employer and the bargaining
agent for the bargaining unit determined under section 238.14. The
process for the resolution of those disputes is arbitration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Restriction on Content of Collective Agreement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406577 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

**[[238.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.19 .sectionLabel}** A collective
agreement that applies to the bargaining unit determined under section
238.14 must not, directly or indirectly, alter or eliminate any existing
term or condition of employment or establish any new term or condition
of employment if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} doing so would require the enactment or amendment of
  any legislation by Parliament, except for the purpose of appropriating
  money required for the implementation of the term or condition; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the term or condition is one that has been or may be
  established under the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/R-11), the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Pension
  Continuation Act](/eng/acts/R-10.6), the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01), the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36) or the [Government Employees Compensation
  Act](/eng/acts/G-5).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Essential Services, Conciliation and Strike Votes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406584 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of Divisions 8, 10 and 11
of Part 1

**[[238.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.2 .sectionLabel}** Divisions 8, 10 and
11 of Part 1 do not apply in respect of the bargaining unit determined
under section 238.14.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Arbitration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406589 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitral award --- additional factor

**[[238.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.21 .sectionLabel}** In addition to
the factors set out in section 148, the arbitration board may, if
relevant to making a determination under that section in regards to a
collective agreement that applies to the bargaining unit determined
under section 238.14, take into account the impact of the determination
on the operational effectiveness of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
- 2018, c. 24, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238.21-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 238.21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitral award --- limitations

- **[[238.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The arbitral award that applies to
  the bargaining unit determined under section 238.14 must not, directly
  or indirectly, alter or eliminate any existing term or condition of
  employment, or establish any new term or condition of employment, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} doing so would require the enactment or amendment
    of any legislation by Parliament, except for the purpose of
    appropriating money required for the implementation of the term or
    condition;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the term or condition is one that has been or may
    be established under the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
    Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/R-11), the [Royal Canadian Mounted
    Police Pension Continuation Act](/eng/acts/R-10.6), the [Public
    Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01), the [Public Service
    Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36) or the [Government Employees
    Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} doing so would affect either of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the organization of the public service, the
      categories of members as defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Royal
      Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) or the assignment of
      duties to, and the classification of, positions and persons
      employed in the public service, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the right or authority of the Commissioner of
      the Royal Canadian Mounted Police under the [Royal Canadian
      Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) to ensure that police
      operations are effective.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection 150(2) applies in
  respect of the arbitral award.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406606 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[238.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.23 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the oath of secrecy referred to in
  subsection 238.07(5); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the measures that are to be taken by the
  Board or a party to protect the information referred to in subsection
  238.07(1) that the Board or the party has received from the RCMP
  Commissioner in connection with any matter or proceeding before the
  Board under this Division or Part 1 that concerns a person who is or
  was an RCMP member or a reservist, and respecting the disclosure of
  that information by the Board or the party to other persons or
  entities.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Grievances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406613 .Subheading}

#### [Individual Grievances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406614 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited right to grieve

**[[238.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.24 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsections 208(2) to (7), an employee who is an RCMP member is entitled
to present an individual grievance only if they feel aggrieved by the
interpretation or application, in respect of the employee, of a
provision of a collective agreement or arbitral award.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited right to refer to adjudication

- **[[238.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee who is an RCMP member
  may refer to adjudication an individual grievance that has been
  presented up to and including the final level in the grievance process
  and that has not been dealt with to the employee's satisfaction only
  if the grievance is related to the interpretation or application, in
  respect of the employee, of a provision of a collective agreement or
  arbitral award.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before referring an individual grievance to
  adjudication, the employee must obtain the approval of their
  bargaining agent to represent the employee in the adjudication
  proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grievance related to accessibility

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a grievance referred to in subsection (1) is
  related to the contravention of a provision of regulations made under
  subsection 117(1) of the [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6), an
  employee who is an RCMP member may refer the grievance to adjudication
  only if the employee has suffered physical or psychological harm,
  property damage or economic loss as a result of --- or has otherwise
  been adversely affected by --- the contravention.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
- [2019, c. 10, s. 193](#1154822-1191897){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154822-1191897.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238.25-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 238.25"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Adjournment of Proceedings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406626 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prejudice to ongoing investigations or
proceedings

- **[[238.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An adjudicator or the Board must, on
  the request of the RCMP Commissioner or the employer, adjourn all
  proceedings in relation to any matter before the adjudicator or the
  Board under this Division or Part 2 that concerns a person who is or
  was an RCMP member or a reservist, if the adjudicator or the Board is
  satisfied that to continue those proceedings would prejudice an
  ongoing criminal investigation or an ongoing criminal or civil
  proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum period of adjournment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No adjournment may be for a period of more than 90
  days. However, the RCMP Commissioner or the employer may request more
  than one adjournment in relation to a matter.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Law Enforcement, Public Safety and National Security]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406634 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-disclosure

- **[[238.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The RCMP Commissioner may, in
  connection with any matter or proceeding before an adjudicator or the
  Board under this Division or Part 2 that concerns a person who is or
  was an RCMP member or a reservist, object to the disclosure to the
  adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, or to a party of any
  information whose disclosure would, in that Commissioner's opinion, be
  injurious to law enforcement, public safety or national security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of objection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the RCMP Commissioner objects to the disclosure of
  information under subsection (1), he or she must, as soon as feasible,
  give written notice of the objection and the reasons for it to the
  adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, and to the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other provisions do not apply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The RCMP Commissioner may object to the disclosure of
  information under subsection (1) despite any provision of this Act or
  the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations and Employment Board
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.35) that would otherwise permit the adjudicator or
  the Board, as the case may be, to require that the information be
  disclosed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former judge

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the RCMP Commissioner objects to the disclosure of
  information under subsection (1), the Minister must, at the written
  request of the adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, or a
  party, appoint a former judge of a superior court of a province or the
  Federal Court to review the information and to make an order under
  section 238.28.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath of secrecy

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In order to be appointed, the former judge must
  obtain a security clearance from the Government of Canada and take the
  oath of secrecy prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of appointment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must provide notice to the adjudicator
  or the Board, as the case may be, to the parties and to the RCMP
  Commissioner when a former judge has been appointed in accordance with
  subsection (4). The adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, the
  parties and the RCMP Commissioner may make their representations to
  the former judge only within 30 days after the day on which the notice
  is sent or any longer period, not more than 60 days after the day on
  which the notice is sent, that the former judge may permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former judge to have access

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The former judge is to have access to the information
  about which an objection is made under subsection (1) for the purposes
  of the review.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order regarding disclosure

- **[[238.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the former judge concludes
  that the disclosure of the information about which an objection is
  made under subsection 238.27(1) would be injurious to law enforcement,
  public safety or national security, the former judge must order that
  the RCMP Commissioner's objection under section 238.27 to the
  disclosure of that information be dismissed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the former judge concludes that the disclosure of
  all or part of that information would be injurious to law enforcement,
  public safety or national security but that the public interest in
  disclosure outweighs in importance the public interest in
  non-disclosure, the former judge, after considering the public
  interest in disclosure, must order the RCMP Commissioner to disclose
  to the adjudicator, to the Board or to the party, as the case may be,
  all or that part of that information, a summary of that information or
  a written admission of facts relating to that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No disclosure required

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the former judge does not make an order under
  subsection (1) or order disclosure under subsection (2), they must
  make an order upholding the RCMP Commissioner's objection to the
  disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The order of the former judge must be made within 30
  days after the day on which the period referred to in subsection
  238.27(6) expires or within any longer period, not more than 60 days
  after the day on which the period referred to in that subsection
  expires, that the Minister permits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The former judge must not include in their order any
  information that reveals information that they conclude must not be
  disclosed, or any information from which that information may be
  inferred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order final

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The former judge's order is final and binding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The RCMP Commissioner's objection is not subject to
  review except to the extent and in the manner provided under this
  section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection

- **[[238.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No criminal, civil or administrative
  action or proceeding lies against a former judge, or any person acting
  on their behalf or under their direction, for anything done, reported
  or said in good faith in the exercise or purported exercise of any
  power, or the performance or purported performance of any duty or
  function, of the former judge under sections 238.27 and 238.28.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No summons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The former judge, or any person acting on their
  behalf or under their direction, is not, in respect of any matter
  coming to the knowledge of the former judge or that person as a result
  of exercising a power or performing a duty or function of the former
  judge, a competent or compellable witness in any proceeding other than
  a prosecution for an offence under this Act, the [Foreign Interference
  and Security of Information Act](/eng/acts/O-5) or section 132 or 136
  of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
- [2024, c. 16, s. 57](#1460885-1486948){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1460885-1486948.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238.29-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 238.29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of information

**[[238.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.3 .sectionLabel}** Information about
which an objection is made under subsection 238.27(1) that is disclosed
by the RCMP Commissioner to an adjudicator or the Board, as the case may
be, or to a party in connection with the matter or proceeding that gave
rise to the objection may be used only in connection with that matter or
proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Memorandum of understanding

**[[238.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.31 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson
and the RCMP Commissioner may enter into a memorandum of understanding
setting out principles and procedures respecting the disclosure of
information referred to in subsection 238.27(1) and the protection of
that information if it is disclosed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information --- other legal
proceedings

- **[[238.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the RCMP Commissioner discloses
  information about which an objection is made under subsection
  238.27(1) to an adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, or to a
  party in connection with the matter or proceeding that gave rise to
  the objection, the adjudicator, the Board or that party is not to be
  required, in connection with any other criminal, civil or
  administrative action or proceeding, to give or produce evidence
  relating to that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided by any other Act of Parliament
  that expressly refers to it, this section applies despite any other
  Act of Parliament, other than the [Access to Information
  Act](/eng/acts/A-1) and the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Section prevails

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section applies despite subsection 13(1) of the
  [Auditor General Act](/eng/acts/A-17) and subsection 79.3(1) of the
  [Parliament of Canada Act](/eng/acts/P-1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Unique Role as Police Organization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406693 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration

**[[238.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.33 .sectionLabel}** In considering a
grievance that concerns an employee who is an RCMP member or a
reservist, the adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, must take
into account the unique role of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police as a
police organization in protecting public safety and national security
and its need to deploy its members and reservists as it sees fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-406698 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[238.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.34 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the oath of secrecy referred to in
  subsection 238.27(5); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the measures that are to be taken by an
  adjudicator or the Board, as the case may be, or a party to protect
  the information referred to in subsection 238.27(1) that the
  adjudicator, the Board or the party has received from the RCMP
  Commissioner in connection with any matter or proceeding before the
  adjudicator or the Board under this Division or Part 2 that concerns a
  person who is or was an RCMP member or a reservist, and respecting the
  disclosure of that information by the adjudicator, the Board or the
  party to other persons or entities.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 9, s. 33
:::

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Occupational Health and Safety]{.HTitleText1} {#h-406705 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406706 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of public service

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, [[public
service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in subsection
11(1) of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

### [Part II of Canada Labour Code]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406709 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to public service

**[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** Part II of the [Canada
Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) applies to and in respect of the public
service and persons employed in it as if the public service were a
federal work, undertaking or business referred to in that Part except
that, for the purpose of that application,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any reference in that Part to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} "arbitration" is to be read as a reference to
    adjudication under Part 2 or Division 2 of Part 2.1,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of sections 133 and 134 of the
    [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
    is to be read as a reference to the Public Service Labour Relations
    and Employment Board,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a "collective agreement" is to be read as a
    reference to a collective agreement within the meaning of subsection
    2(1),

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} "employee" is to be read as a reference to a
    person employed in the public service, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a "trade union" is to be read as a reference to an
    employee organization within the meaning of subsection 2(1) and
    includes, unless the context otherwise requires, a council of
    employee organizations within the meaning of that subsection;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 20, s. 396\]]{.Repealed}

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of this Act apply, with any necessary
  modifications, in respect of matters brought before the Federal Public
  Sector Labour Relations and Employment Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "240"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 384
- 2017, c. 9, ss. 34, 57
- [2017, c. 20, s. 396](#1037156-1193463){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1037156-1193463.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 240"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-406722 .Part}

### [Defects in Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406723 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defect in form or irregularity

- **[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  proceeding under this Act is invalid by reason only of a defect in
  form or a technical irregularity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grievance process

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The failure to present a grievance at all required
  levels in accordance with the applicable grievance process is not a
  defect in form or a technical irregularity for the purposes of
  subsection (1).

### [Restriction on Admissibility of Evidence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406729 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** Except in the case of a
prosecution for perjury,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} testimony or proceedings before an arbitration board
  are not admissible in evidence in any court in Canada; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reports of a public interest commission, and
  testimony or proceedings before a public interest commission, are not
  admissible in evidence in any court in Canada.

### [Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406734 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence respecting information obtained

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** Members of arbitration
boards, members of public interest commissions, mediators, adjudicators
and persons seized of referrals under subsection 182(1) are not
competent or compellable to appear as witnesses in any civil action,
suit or other proceeding respecting information obtained in the
discharge of their functions under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "243"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 385
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-243-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 243"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No disclosure of notes and drafts

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** The following may not
be disclosed without the consent of the person who made them:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} notes or draft orders or decisions of an adjudicator;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notes or draft reports of a mediator, a public
  interest commission or a person authorized or designated by the Board
  to assist in resolving a complaint or issue in dispute before the
  Board; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} notes or a draft arbitral award of an arbitration
  board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "244"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 386
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-244-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 244"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criminal or civil proceedings

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** No criminal or civil
proceedings lie against a member of an arbitration board, a member of a
public interest commission, a mediator, an adjudicator or a person
seized of a referral under subsection 182(1) for anything done  ---  or
omitted to be done  ---  or reported or said in good faith in the course
of the exercise or performance or purported exercise or performance of
any power, duty or function under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "245"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 387
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-245-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 245"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Oaths and Solemn Affirmations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406750 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath or solemn affirmation

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** Before commencing his
or her functions, a person appointed under this Act must take an oath or
make a solemn affirmation in the following form before a commissioner of
oaths or other person having authority to administer oaths or solemn
affirmations:

I, \...\...\...\...\...\....., do swear (or solemnly affirm) that I will
faithfully, truly and impartially, to the best of my judgment, skill and
ability, execute and perform the office of \...\...\...\...\...\...\...

### [Remuneration and Expenses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406753 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

- **[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Members of arbitration boards,
  mediators, adjudicators and persons seized of referrals under
  subsection 182(1) are entitled to be paid the remuneration and
  expenses that may be fixed by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Members of public interest commissions are entitled
  to be paid the remuneration and expenses that may be fixed by the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Party to pay

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a public interest commission consists of three
  members, the remuneration and expenses to be paid to the member of the
  commission nominated, or deemed to be nominated, by a party is to be
  paid by that party.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "247"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 337
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-247-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 247"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Witness Fees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406763 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of witness fees

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
summoned by an arbitration board, a public interest commission or an
adjudicator to attend as a witness at any proceeding under this Act is
entitled to receive fees and allowances for so attending that are equal
to those to which the person would be entitled if summoned to attend
before the Federal Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "248"
- 2013, c. 40, s. 389
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-248-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 248"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Provision of Facilities and Human Resources]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406768 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facilities and human resources

**[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Administrator
of the Administrative Tribunals Support Service of Canada must provide
members of arbitration boards, members of public interest commissions,
mediators, adjudicators and persons seized of referrals under subsection
182(1) with the facilities and human resources necessary to enable them
to carry out their functions under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 22, s. 2 "249"
- 2014, c. 20, s. 481
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 249"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application of Safety or Security Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406773 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of safety or security
provisions

- **[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Act or any other Act
  is to be construed as requiring the employer to do or refrain from
  doing anything that is contrary to any instruction, direction or
  regulation given or made by or on behalf of the Government of Canada
  in the interest of the safety or security of Canada or of any state
  allied or associated with Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order is conclusive proof

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), an order made by
  the Governor in Council is conclusive proof of the matters stated in
  it in relation to the giving or making of any instruction, direction
  or regulation by or on behalf of the Government of Canada in the
  interest of the safety or security of Canada or any state allied or
  associated with Canada.

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2013, c.
40, s. 390\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-251-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 251"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Five-year Review]{.HTitleText2} {#h-406780 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

**[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** Five years after the
day on which this section comes into force, the minister designated by
the Governor in Council for the purposes of this section must cause a
review of this Act and its administration and operation to be conducted,
and cause a report on the review to be laid before each House of
Parliament on any of the first 15 days on which that House is sitting
after the review is completed.
