## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 36

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions .MarginalNote}

    - [36]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this Division.

      [[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chairperson of
          the new Board. ([président]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Public Service
          Staff Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-35), being chapter P-35 of
          the Revised Statutes of Canada, 1985. ([ancienne
          loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[former Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[former Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
          Staff Relations Board established by section 11 of the former
          Act. ([ancienne Commission]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[new Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[new Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
          Labour Relations Act, enacted by section 2 of this
          Act. ([nouvelle loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[new Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[new Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
          Labour Relations Board established by section 12 of the new
          Act. ([nouvelle Commission]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    - ###### Meaning of other words {#meaning-of-other-words .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Words and expressions used in this Division have
      the same meaning as in the former Act or the new Act, as the
      context requires.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 37

  - ###### Certain members continue {#certain-members-continue .MarginalNote}

    [37]{.lawlabel} Each member of the former Board, other than the
    Deputy Chairpersons, holding office immediately before the day on
    which section 12 of the new Act comes into force continues to hold
    office and is deemed to have been appointed under that section to
    hold office for the remainder of the term for which he or she had
    been appointed before the coming into force of that section.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 38

  - ###### Deputy Chairpersons {#deputy-chairpersons .MarginalNote}

    [38]{.lawlabel} The Deputy Chairpersons of the former Board cease to
    hold office on the day on which section 12 of the new Act comes into
    force.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 39

  - ###### Transfer of proceedings {#transfer-of-proceedings .MarginalNote}

    - [39]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Division, any
      proceeding that the former Board was seized of immediately before
      the day on which section 12 of the new Act comes into force is
      transferred to the new Board to be disposed of in accordance with
      the new Act.

    - ###### Continuing jurisdiction of Deputy Chairperson {#continuing-jurisdiction-of-deputy-chairperson .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Deputy Chairperson of the former Board may, at
      the request of the Chairperson, continue to hear, consider or
      decide any matter that was before the Deputy Chairperson before
      the day on which section 12 of the new Act comes into force and in
      respect of which there was any proceeding in which he or she
      participated.

    - ###### Powers {#powers .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), a Deputy
      Chairperson may exercise any of the powers of a panel of the new
      Board.

    - ###### Refusal to complete duties {#refusal-to-complete-duties .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a Deputy Chairperson who was a member of a
      panel refuses to continue to hear, consider or decide any matter
      referred to in subsection (2), the chairperson of the panel may
      continue to hear, consider or decide the matter or the Chairperson
      may remove that matter from the panel and hear, consider or decide
      that matter or assign a Vice-Chairperson or a panel of the new
      Board to do so on any terms and conditions that the Chairperson
      may specify for the protection and preservation of the rights and
      interests of the parties.

    - ###### Supervision by Chairperson {#supervision-by-chairperson .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson has supervision over and
      direction of the work of any Deputy Chairperson who continues to
      hear, consider or decide a matter under subsection (2).

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 40

  - ###### Fees and expenses {#fees-and-expenses .MarginalNote}

    [40]{.lawlabel} A Deputy Chairperson of the former Board who
    continues to hear, consider or decide a matter under subsection
    39(2)

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is to be paid the fees for his or her services
      that may be fixed by the Governor in Council; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to be paid reasonable travel and
      living expenses incurred in the course of providing services
      during any period of absence from his or her ordinary place of
      residence.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 41

  - ###### Limitation period {#limitation-period .MarginalNote}

    [41]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may withdraw from a Deputy
    Chairperson of the former Board any matter referred to in subsection
    39(2) that is not disposed of within one year after the day on which
    section 12 of the new Act comes into force and determine the matter
    or assign it to a panel of the new Board on any terms and conditions
    that the Chairperson may specify for the protection and preservation
    of the rights and interests of the parties.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 42

  - ###### Secretary of former Board {#secretary-of-former-board .MarginalNote}

    - [42]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The person who, immediately
      before the day on which section 48 of the new Act comes into
      force, held the office of secretary of the former Board is deemed
      to have been appointed to the office of Executive Director of the
      new Board, without prejudice to any salary and benefits he or she
      may receive by virtue of having held that office before that day.

    - ###### Officers and employees {#officers-and-employees .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in the new Act affects the status of any
      person who was an officer or employee of the former Board
      immediately before the day on which section 49 of the new Act
      comes into force, except that, as of that day, the person is an
      officer or employee, as the case may be, of the new Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 43

  - ###### Rights and obligations transferred {#rights-and-obligations-transferred .MarginalNote}

    [43]{.lawlabel} All rights and property held by or in the name of or
    in trust for the former Board and all obligations and liabilities of
    the former Board are deemed to be rights, property, obligations and
    liabilities of the new Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 44

  - ###### References {#references .MarginalNote}

    [44]{.lawlabel} Every reference to the former Board in a deed,
    contract or other document executed by the former Board in its own
    name is to be read as a reference to the new Board, unless the
    context otherwise requires.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 45

  - ###### Transfer of appropriations {#transfer-of-appropriations .MarginalNote}

    [45]{.lawlabel} Any amount appropriated, for the fiscal year that
    includes the day on which section 12 of the new Act comes into
    force, by an appropriation Act based on the Estimates for that year
    for defraying the charges and expenses of the public service of
    Canada for the former Board and that, on that day, is unexpended is
    deemed, on that day, to be an amount appropriated for defraying the
    charges and expenses of the public service of Canada for the new
    Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 46

  - ###### Continuation of legal proceedings {#continuation-of-legal-proceedings .MarginalNote}

    [46]{.lawlabel} Any action, suit or other proceeding, to which the
    former Board is a party, that is pending in any court on the day on
    which section 12 of the new Act comes into force may be continued by
    or against the new Board in the like manner and to the same extent
    as it could have been continued by or against the former Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 47

  - ###### Decisions, etc., continued {#decisions-etc.-continued .MarginalNote}

    [47]{.lawlabel} Every decision, order, determination and declaration
    made by the former Board is deemed to have been made by the new
    Board and may be enforced as such.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 48

  - ###### Certification continued {#certification-continued .MarginalNote}

    - [48]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each employee organization that,
      immediately before the day on which section 64 of the new Act
      comes into force, was certified as the bargaining agent for a
      bargaining unit continues to be certified as the bargaining agent
      for the bargaining unit.

    - ###### Effects of certification {#effects-of-certification .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 67 of the new Act applies as though the
      employee organization had been certified under the new Act.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 49

  - ###### Legal officers {#legal-officers .MarginalNote}

    - [49]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the new Act,
      including any application under section 58 of the new Act, an
      employee who, on or after the day on which the definition
      [[managerial or confidential position]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
      subsection 2(1) of that Act comes into force, is employed as a
      legal officer in the Department of Justice or the Canada Customs
      and Revenue Agency is deemed not to be included in any unit
      determined, in accordance with the former Act, to constitute a
      unit of employees appropriate for collective bargaining.

    - ###### New application required {#new-application-required .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, any employee organization
      that wishes to represent employees in a bargaining unit that
      includes one or more employees referred to in subsection (1) must
      proceed by way of an application under section 54 of the new Act.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 50

  - ###### Certain positions continued {#certain-positions-continued .MarginalNote}

    [50]{.lawlabel} Every position that was a position referred to in
    any of paragraphs (a), (b), (e), (f) and (g) of the definition
    [[managerial or confidential position]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
    subsection 2(1) of the former Act immediately before the day on
    which the definition [[managerial or confidential
    position]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1) of the new Act
    comes into force is deemed, as of that day, to be a managerial or
    confidential position within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the
    new Act.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 51

  - ###### Process for resolution of disputes {#process-for-resolution-of-disputes .MarginalNote}

    [51]{.lawlabel} The process for resolution of a dispute specified by
    a bargaining agent for a bargaining unit and recorded by the former
    Board continues to be the process applicable to that bargaining unit
    until it is changed in accordance with the new Act.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 52

  - ###### Collective agreements {#collective-agreements .MarginalNote}

    [52]{.lawlabel} Every collective agreement entered into under the
    former Act that is in force immediately before the day on which the
    definition [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
    subsection 2(1) of the new Act comes into force is deemed to be a
    collective agreement entered into under the new Act and continues in
    force until its term expires.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 53

  - ###### Arbitral awards {#arbitral-awards .MarginalNote}

    [53]{.lawlabel} Every arbitral award made under the former Act that
    is in force immediately before the day on which the definition
    [[arbitral award]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1) of the
    new Act comes into force is deemed to be an arbitral award made
    under the new Act and continues in force until its term expires.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 54

  - ###### Conciliators {#conciliators .MarginalNote}

    [54]{.lawlabel} Every person appointed as a conciliator under
    section 53 of the former Act before the day on which that section is
    repealed may continue to act after that day in accordance with that
    section and section 54 of the former Act, as those sections read
    immediately before that day, except that the references to
    "Chairperson" in section 54 of the former Act are to be read as
    references to the Chairperson of the new Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 55

  - ###### Fact finders {#fact-finders .MarginalNote}

    [55]{.lawlabel} Every person appointed as a fact finder under
    section 54.1 of the former Act before the day on which that section
    is repealed may continue to act after that day in accordance with
    section 54.4 of the former Act, as that section read immediately
    before that day and sections 54.1 to 54.6 of the former Act, as
    those sections read immediately before that day, apply after that
    day to the parties being assisted by the fact finder, except that
    the references in those sections to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} "Board" are to be read as references to the new
      Board; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} "Chairperson" are to be read as references to the
      Chairperson of the new Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 56

  - ###### Section 61 of the former Act {#section-61-of-the-former-act .MarginalNote}

    [56]{.lawlabel} Every person to whom terms and conditions were
    referred under section 61 of the former Act before the day on which
    that section is repealed may continue to act after that day in
    accordance with the provisions of the former Act as they read
    immediately before that day.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 57

  - ###### Arbitration {#arbitration .MarginalNote}

    - [57]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following rules apply to
      requests for arbitration made before the day on which section 136
      of the new Act comes into force and for which no arbitral award
      had been made before that day:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if no arbitration board had been established or
        arbitrator appointed before that day, the request is to be dealt
        with as though it had been made under section 136 of the new
        Act;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an arbitrator had been appointed before that
        day, the arbitrator is deemed to be an arbitration board
        consisting of a single member established under section 139 of
        the new Act and the arbitration is to continue in accordance
        with Division 9 of Part 1 of the new Act; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if an arbitration board had been established
        before that day, the arbitration board is deemed to be an
        arbitration board consisting of three members established under
        section 140 of the new Act and the arbitration is to continue in
        accordance with Division 9 of Part 1 of the new Act.

    - ###### Restriction {#restriction .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, an arbitral award may be
      made under subsection (1) only in respect of a term or condition
      of employment that could have been embodied in an arbitral award
      made under the former Act as it read immediately before the day on
      which section 140 of the new Act comes into force.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 58

  - ###### Provisions of former Act apply {#provisions-of-former-act-apply .MarginalNote}

    - [58]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, before the day on which
      section 105 of the new Act comes into force, a notice to bargain
      collectively was given under the former Act in respect of a
      bargaining unit for which the process for resolution of a dispute
      is conciliation and no collective agreement was entered into
      before that day as a result of the notice, paragraphs (a) to (c)
      apply to the employer, the bargaining agent for the bargaining
      unit and every employee in the bargaining unit until a collective
      agreement is entered into:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 76 to 90.1 and sections 102 to 107 of
        the former Act, as those sections read immediately before that
        day, apply on and after that day, except that references in
        those sections to

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} "Board" are to be read as references to the
          new Board,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} "Chairperson" are to be read as references
          to the Chairperson of the new Board, and

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} "Minister" are to be read as references to
          the Minister within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the new
          Act;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a designation review panel established before
        that day may continue to act on and after that day; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a conciliation commissioner appointed or a
        conciliation board established before that day may continue to
        act on and after that day.

    - ###### Non-application {#non-application .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, if subsection (1) applies
      to the employer, the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit and
      every employee in the bargaining unit, Divisions 8, 10, 11 and 14
      of Part 1 of the new Act do not apply to them until a collective
      agreement is entered into.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 59

  - ###### Complaints referred to in par. 23(1)(b) of former Act {#complaints-referred-to-in-par.-231b-of-former-act .MarginalNote}

    [59]{.lawlabel} Every complaint referred to in paragraph 23(1)(b) of
    the former Act that was not finally disposed of by the former Board
    before the day on which section 221 of the new Act comes into force
    is deemed, for the purpose of the new Act, to be a policy grievance
    that has been referred to adjudication and, if a panel of the former
    Board had commenced to hear the complaint, that panel is, subject to
    section 39, deemed to be an adjudicator or a board of adjudication,
    as the case may be.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 60

  - ###### Complaints referred to in par. 23(1)(c) of former Act {#complaints-referred-to-in-par.-231c-of-former-act .MarginalNote}

    [60]{.lawlabel} Complaints referred to in paragraph 23(1)(c) of the
    former Act that were not finally disposed of by the former Board
    before the day on which section 234 of the new Act comes into force
    are deemed to have been withdrawn immediately before that day.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 61

  - ###### Former Act applies {#former-act-applies .MarginalNote}

    - [61]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), every
      grievance presented in accordance with the former Act that was not
      finally dealt with before the day on which section 208 of the new
      Act comes into force is to be dealt with on and after that day in
      accordance with the provisions of the former Act, as they read
      immediately before that day.

    - ###### Adjudicators {#adjudicators .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), an
      adjudicator under the former Act may continue to hear, consider or
      decide any grievance referred to him or her before the day on
      which section 209 of the new Act comes into force, except that if
      the adjudicator was a member of the former Board, he or she may do
      so only if requested to do so by the Chairperson.

    - ###### Supervision by Chairperson {#supervision-by-chairperson-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson has supervision over and
      direction of the work of any member of the former Board who
      continues to hear, consider or decide a grievance under subsection
      (2).

    - ###### Refusal to complete duties {#refusal-to-complete-duties-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} If an adjudicator under the former Act refuses to
      continue to hear, consider or decide a grievance referred to in
      subsection (2), the Chairperson may, on any terms and conditions
      that the Chairperson may specify for the protection and
      preservation of the rights and interests of the parties, refer the
      grievance to a member of the new Board.

    - ###### Appointment after commencement day {#appointment-after-commencement-day .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a grievance referred to in subsection (1) is
      referred to adjudication after the day on which section 209 of the
      new Act comes into force, the provisions of the new Act apply with
      respect to the appointment of the adjudicator.

    - ###### Powers {#powers-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (2) and (5), the
      adjudicator may exercise any of the powers an adjudicator under
      the former Act could have exercised under that Act.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 62

  - ###### Fees and expenses {#fees-and-expenses-1 .MarginalNote}

    [62]{.lawlabel} Each adjudicator under the former Act who continues
    to hear, consider or decide a grievance under subsection 61(2) who,
    at the time of his or her appointment, was a member of the former
    Board or an adjudicator referred to in paragraph 95(2)(a.1) of the
    former Act

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is to be paid the fees that may be fixed by the
      Governor in Council; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to be paid reasonable travel and
      living expenses incurred while doing so during any period of
      absence from his or her ordinary place of residence.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 63

  - ###### Limitation period {#limitation-period-1 .MarginalNote}

    [63]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may withdraw from any member of the
    former Board any grievance referred to in subsection 61(2) that is
    not disposed of within one year after the day on which section 209
    of the new Act comes into force and the Chairperson may, on any
    terms and conditions that he or she may specify for the protection
    and preservation of the rights and interests of the parties, refer
    the matter to a member of the new Board.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 64

  - ###### Events giving rise to individual grievances {#events-giving-rise-to-individual-grievances .MarginalNote}

    [64]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations made under section 237 of the
    new Act or any applicable collective agreement or arbitral award, an
    individual grievance may be presented on or after the day on which
    section 208 of the new Act comes into force in respect of any event
    that occurred before that day and that would have given rise to a
    right to grieve under section 91 of the former Act, as that section
    read immediately before that day.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 65

  - ###### Events giving rise to policy grievances {#events-giving-rise-to-policy-grievances .MarginalNote}

    [65]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations made under section 237 of the
    new Act or any applicable collective agreement or arbitral award, a
    policy grievance may be presented on or after the day on which
    section 220 of the new Act comes into force in respect of any event
    that occurred before that day if the matter could have been referred
    to the former Board under section 99 of the former Act, as that
    section read immediately before that day.

- --- 2003, c. 22, s. 66

  - ###### Former adjudication orders {#former-adjudication-orders .MarginalNote}

    [66]{.lawlabel} Every order made by an adjudicator under the former
    Act is deemed to be an order made by an adjudicator under the new
    Act and may be enforced as such.

- --- 2009, c. 2, s. 396(3), as amended by 2018, c. 27, s. 431(2)

  - ###### Powers of Board {#powers-of-board .MarginalNote}

    - [396]{.lawlabel} [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board has, in relation to a
      complaint referred to it, in addition to the powers conferred on
      it under the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), the power to interpret and apply sections
      7, 10 and 11 of the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6),
      and the Equal Wages Guidelines, 1986, in respect of employees,
      even after the coming into force of subsection 425(1) of the
      Budget Implementation Act 2018, No. 2.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 338, as amended by 2014, c. 20, s. 309

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions-1 .MarginalNote}

    - [338]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this section.

      [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the day on
          which this Act receives royal assent. ([date de
          référence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[the Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[the Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
          Labour Relations Act. ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    - ###### Words and expressions {#words-and-expressions .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the context otherwise requires, words and
      expressions used in this section have the same meaning as in the
      Act.

    - ###### Application of provisions enacted by this Act {#application-of-provisions-enacted-by-this-act .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) to (7), the provisions
      of the Act, as enacted by sections 294 to 306, subsection 307(1),
      sections 308 to 314, subsection 316(1) and sections 317 to 324
      also apply in respect of the following bargaining units:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a bargaining unit in respect of which a notice
        to commence bargaining collectively with a view to entering
        into, renewing or revising a collective agreement has been given
        before the commencement day;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a bargaining unit that is bound by a collective
        agreement or arbitral award that is in force on the commencement
        day and that expires on that day or at any time after that day,
        and in respect of which no notice to bargain collectively has
        been given before the commencement day; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a bargaining unit in respect of which no notice
        to bargain collectively for the purpose of entering into a first
        collective agreement has been given before the commencement day.

    - ###### Arbitration board established {#arbitration-board-established .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (6) and (7), the
      provisions of the Act, as they read immediately before the
      commencement day, continue to apply in respect of a bargaining
      unit that is referred to in paragraph (3)(a) until an arbitral
      award is made in respect of the bargaining unit if, before the
      commencement day,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a request for arbitration was made by the
        employer or the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Chairperson had notified the parties of the
        establishment of an arbitration board.

    - ###### Public interest commission established {#public-interest-commission-established .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (6) and (7), the
      provisions of the Act, as they read immediately before the
      commencement day, continue to apply in respect of a bargaining
      unit that is referred to in paragraph (3)(a) until a collective
      agreement is entered into by parties if, before the commencement
      day,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a request for conciliation was made by the
        employer or the bargaining agent for the bargaining unit; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Chairperson had notified the parties of the
        establishment of a public interest commission.

    - ###### No essential services agreement {#no-essential-services-agreement .MarginalNote}

      [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (4) and (5), if, before the
      commencement day, arbitration or conciliation has been chosen by
      the bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit that is
      referred to in paragraph (3)(a) or (b) as the process for the
      resolution of disputes to which it may be a party and if, before
      that day, no essential services agreement has been entered into by
      the employer and the bargaining agent in relation to that
      bargaining unit, then,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if no notification referred to in paragraph
        (4)(b) or (5)(b) had been given before December 12, 2013, the
        process for the resolution of disputes is conciliation; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 120 and 121 of the Act, as enacted by
        section 305, apply except that, despite subsection 121(3) of the
        Act, as enacted by section 305, the notice that is referred to
        in subsection 121(1) of the Act, as enacted by section 305, must
        be given not later than 12 months after the commencement day.

    - ###### Essential services agreement entered into {#essential-services-agreement-entered-into .MarginalNote}

      [(7)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (4) and (5), if, before the
      commencement day, arbitration or conciliation has been chosen by
      the bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit that is
      referred to in paragraph (3)(a) or (b) as the process for the
      resolution of disputes to which it may be a party and if, before
      that day, an essential services agreement has been entered into by
      the employer and the bargaining agent in relation to that
      bargaining unit, then, the process for the resolution of disputes
      is

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} arbitration, if 80% or more of the positions in
        the bargaining unit were, immediately before the commencement
        day, necessary for the employer to provide an essential service;
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} conciliation, if less than 80% of the positions
        in the bargaining unit were, immediately before the commencement
        day, necessary for the employer to provide an essential service.

    - ###### Non-application {#non-application-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (7) does not apply if the
      notification referred to in paragraph (4)(b) or (5)(b) had been
      given before December 12, 2013.

    - ###### Application of subsection 105(2) {#application-of-subsection-1052 .MarginalNote}

      [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 105(2) of the Act, as that subsection
      read immediately before the commencement day, continues to apply
      in respect of a bargaining unit referred to in paragraph (3)(b)
      until an arbitral award is made or a collective agreement is
      entered into in respect of a bargaining unit.

    - ###### Positions --- essential services agreement {#positions-essential-services-agreement .MarginalNote}

      [(9)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (4) and (5), if a bargaining
      unit is bound by an essential services agreement immediately
      before the commencement day,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 120 and 121 of the Act, as enacted by
        section 305, apply except that, despite subsection 121(3) of the
        Act, as enacted by section 305, the notice that is referred to
        in subsection 121(1) of the Act, as enacted by section 305, must
        be given not later than 12 months after the commencement day;
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every position that is identified in the
        agreement as being necessary for the employer to provide
        essential services is deemed to be a position designated by the
        employer under section 120 of the Act, as enacted by section
        305.

      For greater certainty, subsection 124(1) of the Act, as enacted by
      section 305, does not apply in respect of any position referred to
      in paragraph (b).

    - ###### For greater certainty {#for-greater-certainty .MarginalNote}

      [(10)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every essential services agreement between the
        employer and a bargaining agent that is in force immediately
        before the commencement day is deemed to have ceased to have
        effect on the commencement day; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every essential services agreement that is
        entered into by the employer and a bargaining agent after the
        commencement day ceases to apply on the day on which a
        collective agreement is entered into by the employer and the
        bargaining agent.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 391

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions-2 .MarginalNote}

    [391]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in sections 392 to
    402.

    [[former Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[former Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
        Labour Relations Board that is established by section 12 of the
        Public Service Labour Relations Act, as that Act read
        immediately before the day on which subsection 366(1) of this
        Act comes into force. ([ancienne Commission]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

    [[new Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[new Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
        Labour Relations and Employment Board that is established by
        subsection 4(1) of the Public Service Labour Relations and
        Employment Board Act. ([nouvelle Commission]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 392

  - ###### Appointments terminated {#appointments-terminated .MarginalNote}

    - [392]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Members of the former Board
      cease to hold office on the day on which subsection 366(1) of this
      Act comes into force.

    - ###### No compensation {#no-compensation .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite the provisions of any contract, agreement
      or order, no person who was appointed to hold office as a
      part-time member of the former Board has any right to claim or
      receive any compensation, damages, indemnity or other form of
      relief from Her Majesty in right of Canada or from any employee or
      agent of Her Majesty for ceasing to hold that office or for the
      abolition of that office by the operation of this Division.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 393

  - ###### Continuation of proceedings {#continuation-of-proceedings .MarginalNote}

    [393]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 394, every proceeding commenced
    under the Public Service Labour Relations Act before the day on
    which subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force is to be taken
    up and continued under and in conformity with that Act, as it is
    amended by this Division.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 394

  - ###### Persons employed by Public Service Staffing Tribunal {#persons-employed-by-public-service-staffing-tribunal .MarginalNote}

    [394]{.lawlabel} Every proceeding commenced under Part 1 or 2 of the
    Public Service Labour Relations Act before the day on which
    subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force that relates to a
    person who is employed by the Public Service Staffing Tribunal ---
    continued by subsection 88(1) of the [Public Service Employment
    Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01), as that Act read immediately before the day
    on which subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force --- and that
    was not finally disposed of by the former Board before that day is
    deemed to have been discontinued immediately before that day.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 395

  - ###### Continuation --- members of former Board {#continuation-members-of-former-board .MarginalNote}

    - [395]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} A member of the former Board ---
      other than an adjudicator referred to in paragraph 223(2)(d) of
      the Public Service Labour Relations Act, as that Act read
      immediately before the day on which subsection 366(1) of this Act
      comes into force --- may, at the request of the Chairperson of the
      new Board, continue to hear and decide any matter that was before
      the member before that day.

    - ###### Powers {#powers-2 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a member of
      the former Board exercises the same powers, and performs the same
      duties and functions, as a panel of the new Board.

    - ###### Refusal to complete duties {#refusal-to-complete-duties-2 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a member of the former Board refuses to
      continue to hear or decide any matter referred to in subsection
      (1), the Chairperson of the new Board may assign it to a panel of
      the new Board in accordance with section 37 of the Public Service
      Labour Relations and Employment Board Act on any terms and
      conditions that the Chairperson may specify for the protection and
      preservation of the rights and interests of the parties.

    - ###### Supervision by Chairperson of new Board {#supervision-by-chairperson-of-new-board .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the new Board has supervision
      over and direction of the work of any member of the former Board
      who continues to hear and decide a matter referred to in
      subsection (1).

    - ###### Remuneration and expenses {#remuneration-and-expenses .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} A member of the former Board who continues to
      hear and decide a matter referred to in subsection (1)

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is to be paid the remuneration for their
        services that may be fixed by the Governor in Council; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to be paid reasonable travel and
        living expenses incurred in the course of providing services
        during any period of absence from their ordinary place of
        residence.

    - ###### Limitation {#limitation .MarginalNote}

      [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the new Board may withdraw
      from a member of the former Board a matter referred to in
      subsection (1) that is not disposed of within one year after the
      day on which subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force and
      assign it to a panel of the new Board in accordance with section
      37 of the Public Service Labour Relations and Employment Board Act
      on any terms and conditions that the Chairperson of the new Board
      may specify for the protection and preservation of the rights and
      interests of the parties.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 396

  - ###### Continuation --- former Board member adjudicators {#continuation-former-board-member-adjudicators .MarginalNote}

    - [396]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} An adjudicator referred to in
      paragraph 223(2)(d) of the Public Service Labour Relations Act, as
      that Act read immediately before the day on which subsection
      366(1) of this Act comes into force, may, at the request of the
      Chairperson of the new Board, continue to hear and decide any
      grievance that was before the adjudicator before that day.

    - ###### Powers {#powers-3 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the
      adjudicator exercises the same powers as an adjudicator under the
      Public Service Labour Relations Act, as that Act read immediately
      before the day on which subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into
      force.

    - ###### Refusal to complete duties {#refusal-to-complete-duties-3 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an adjudicator refuses to continue to hear or
      decide a grievance referred to in subsection (1), the new Board is
      seized of the grievance.

    - ###### Supervision by Chairperson of new Board {#supervision-by-chairperson-of-new-board-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the new Board has supervision
      over and direction of the work of any adjudicator who continues to
      hear and decide a grievance referred to in subsection (1).

    - ###### Remuneration and expenses {#remuneration-and-expenses-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} An adjudicator who continues to hear and decide a
      grievance referred to in subsection (1)

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is to be paid the remuneration for their
        services that may be fixed by the Governor in Council; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to be paid reasonable travel and
        living expenses incurred in the course of providing services
        during any period of absence from their ordinary place of
        residence.

    - ###### Limitation {#limitation-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the new Board may withdraw
      from an adjudicator a grievance referred to in subsection (1) that
      is not disposed of within one year after the day on which
      subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force. If the Chairperson
      of the new Board withdraws the grievance, the new Board becomes
      seized of it.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 397

  - ###### Persons employed by former Board {#persons-employed-by-former-board .MarginalNote}

    [397]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Division affects the status of any
    person who was employed by the former Board immediately before the
    day on which subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force, except
    that, as of that day, the person is employed by the new Board.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 398

  - ###### Rights and obligations transferred {#rights-and-obligations-transferred-1 .MarginalNote}

    [398]{.lawlabel} All rights and property held by or in the name of
    or in trust for the former Board and all obligations and liabilities
    of the former Board are deemed to be rights, property, obligations
    and liabilities of the new Board.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 399

  - ###### References {#references-1 .MarginalNote}

    [399]{.lawlabel} Every reference to the former Board in a deed,
    contract or other document executed or, in Quebec, signed by the
    former Board in its own name is to be read as a reference to the new
    Board, unless the context requires otherwise.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 400

  - ###### Continuation of legal proceedings {#continuation-of-legal-proceedings-1 .MarginalNote}

    [400]{.lawlabel} Any action, suit or other proceeding to which the
    former Board is a party that is pending in any court on the day on
    which subsection 366(1) of this Act comes into force may be
    continued by or against the new Board in the same manner and to the
    same extent as it could have been continued by or against the former
    Board.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 401

  - ###### Commencement of legal proceedings {#commencement-of-legal-proceedings .MarginalNote}

    [401]{.lawlabel} Any action, suit or other legal proceeding in
    respect of an obligation or liability incurred by the former Board
    may be brought against the new Board in any court that would have
    had jurisdiction if the action, suit or other legal proceeding had
    been brought against the former Board.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 402

  - ###### Orders and decisions continued {#orders-and-decisions-continued .MarginalNote}

    [402]{.lawlabel} Every order or decision made by the former Board is
    deemed to have been made by the new Board and may be enforced as
    such.

- --- 2017, c. 9, s. 61

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions-3 .MarginalNote}

    - [61]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this section and sections 62 to 64.

      [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Public Service
          Labour Relations Act, as it read immediately before the coming
          into force of section 2. ([ancienne loi]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

      [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
          subsection 2(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
          Act](/eng/acts/R-10). ([membre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[reservist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[reservist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
          appointed as a reservist under regulations made under
          subsection 11(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
          Act](/eng/acts/R-10). ([réserviste]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

    - ###### Same meaning {#same-meaning .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the context requires otherwise, words and
      expressions used in sections 62 to 64 have the same meaning as in
      subsection 2(1) of the former Act.

- --- 2017, c. 9, s. 62

  - ###### Individual grievances {#individual-grievances .MarginalNote}

    - [62]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any individual grievance
      presented by a member under subsection 208(1) of the former Act,
      before the day on which section 238.24 of the [Federal Public
      Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), as enacted by
      section 33, comes into force, that is not related to the
      interpretation or application, in respect of the member, of a
      provision of a collective agreement or arbitral award, is deemed
      never to have been presented, and any decision made in respect of
      such a grievance or any decision made on a review of the decision
      is deemed never to have had effect.

    - ###### Extension of limitation period {#extension-of-limitation-period .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of presenting a grievance or
      taking any other process of redress under the [Royal Canadian
      Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10), and despite any provision of
      that Act, if an individual grievance has been deemed under
      subsection (1) never to have been presented, or if any decision on
      such a grievance has been deemed under that subsection never to
      have had effect, the member who presented the individual grievance
      has, if the subject matter of the grievance or other redress is
      the same as the subject matter of the individual grievance, 30
      days from the day on which section 33 comes into force to present
      that grievance or to take that other process of redress.

    - ###### Limitation {#limitation-2 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies only in the case of an
      individual grievance that, if it had been presented under the
      [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10) or if another
      process of redress under that Act had been taken, would have been
      presented within the time established under that Act to present
      the grievance or take the other process of redress.

- --- 2017, c. 9, s. 63

  - ###### Existing applications for certification {#existing-applications-for-certification .MarginalNote}

    - [63]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, before the day on which
      section 238.13 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), as enacted by section 33, comes into
      force, an employee organization makes an application under section
      54 of the former Act to be certified as bargaining agent for a
      group of employees that includes employees who are members
      appointed to a rank, or employees who are reservists, the employee
      organization must not be certified as bargaining agent for the
      group, unless

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the group consists exclusively of all the
        employees who are members appointed to a rank, other than
        [[officers]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1)
        of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police Act](/eng/acts/R-10), and
        all the employees who are reservists; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee organization --- and, in the case
        of a council of employee organizations, each employee
        organization forming the council --- meets the following
        requirements:

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it has as its primary mandate the
          representation of employees who are members appointed to a
          rank, other than [[officers]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined
          in subsection 2(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
          Act](/eng/acts/R-10),

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} it is not affiliated with a bargaining agent
          or other association that does not have as its primary mandate
          the representation of police officers, and

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} it is not certified as the bargaining agent
          for any other group of employees.

    - ###### Clarification {#clarification .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, for the purposes of
      subsection (1), participation by an employee organization in the
      National Joint Council is not considered to be an affiliation with
      a bargaining agent or other association that does not have as its
      primary mandate the representation of police officers.

    - ###### Certification of no effect {#certification-of-no-effect .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an employee organization is certified as the
      bargaining agent for a bargaining unit contrary to subsection (1),
      that decision or any decision made on a review of the decision is
      deemed never to have had effect.

    - ###### Certification in respect of any other group {#certification-in-respect-of-any-other-group .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, before the day on which section 33 comes into
      force, an employee organization that meets the requirements set
      out in paragraph (1)(b) is certified as the bargaining agent for
      the group described in paragraph (1)(a), any decision made before
      that day in respect of an application made by that employee
      organization to be certified as bargaining agent for any other
      group of employees is deemed never to have had effect.

    - ###### Existing application for certification {#existing-application-for-certification .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, before the day on which section 33 comes into
      force, an employee organization that meets the requirements set
      out in paragraph (1)(b) is certified as the bargaining agent for
      the bargaining unit composed of the group described in paragraph
      (1)(a), on that day, the employee organization's application for
      certification made under section 54 of the former Act is deemed to
      have been an application for certification made under subsection
      238.13(1) of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), as enacted by section 33, instead of under
      that section 54, and that bargaining unit is deemed to have been
      determined under section 238.14 of the [Federal Public Sector
      Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3).

    - ###### Existing application for certification {#existing-application-for-certification-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(6)]{.lawlabel} If, on or after the day on which section 33 comes
      into force, an employee organization that meets the requirements
      set out in paragraph (1)(b) is certified as the bargaining agent
      for the bargaining unit composed of the group described in
      paragraph (1)(a), and the employee organization made the
      application for certification under section 54 of the former Act,
      on being so certified the employee organization's application for
      certification is deemed to have been an application for
      certification made under subsection 238.13(1) of the [Federal
      Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), as enacted
      by section 33, instead of under that section 54, and that
      bargaining unit is deemed to have been determined under section
      238.14 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3).

- --- 2017, c. 9, s. 64

  - ###### Membership in bargaining unit --- members and reservists {#membership-in-bargaining-unit-members-and-reservists .MarginalNote}

    - [64]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any application that is made
      under section 58 of the former Act before the day on which section
      238.16 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), as enacted by section 33, comes into
      force, in regards to whether members appointed to a rank or
      reservists are included in a bargaining unit, other than a
      bargaining unit composed of the group described in paragraph
      63(1)(a), is deemed never to have been made, and any decision made
      in respect of the application or on any review of the decision is
      deemed never to have had effect.

    - ###### Membership in bargaining unit --- other employees {#membership-in-bargaining-unit-other-employees .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any application that is made under section 58 of
      the former Act before the day on which section 238.16 of the
      [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), as
      enacted by section 33, comes into force, in regards to whether any
      employee other than a member appointed to a rank or a reservist is
      included in a bargaining unit composed of the group described in
      paragraph 63(1)(a) for which an employee organization that meets
      the requirements set out in paragraph 63(1)(b) is certified as the
      bargaining agent, is deemed never to have been made, and any
      decision made in respect of the application or on any review of
      the decision is deemed never to have had effect.

- --- 2017, c. 9, s. 65

  - ###### Published date {#published-date .MarginalNote}

    [65]{.lawlabel} As of the date published by the Treasury Board in
    the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) under subsection
    86(1) of the Enhancing Royal Canadian Mounted Police Accountability
    Act, a reference in subsections 63(1) and 64(1) and (2) to a member
    appointed to a rank is to be read as a reference to a member.

- --- 2017, c. 12, s. 16

  - ###### Public Service Labour Relations Act --- existing applications {#public-service-labour-relations-act-existing-applications .MarginalNote}

    [16]{.lawlabel} If the Public Service Labour Relations and
    Employment Board has, during the period beginning on June 16, 2015
    and ending immediately before the day on which section 8 comes into
    force, received an application for certification referred to in
    paragraph 64(1.1)(a) of the Public Service Labour Relations Act or
    an application for a declaration made under subsection 94(1) of that
    Act, and the application has not been finally disposed of before
    that coming into force, that applicationis to be dealt with and
    disposed of in accordance with that Act as it read immediately
    before that coming into force.

- --- 2018, c. 24, s. 27

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions-4 .MarginalNote}

    - [27]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this section.

      [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the day on
          which this Act receives royal assent. ([date de
          référence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[the Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[the Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Federal Public
          Sector Labour Relations
          Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    - ###### Words and expressions {#words-and-expressions-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the context requires otherwise, words and
      expressions used in this section have the same meaning as in the
      Act.

    - [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, before the commencement day, the employer or
      a bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit has filed a
      notice to bargain and neither party has requested arbitration by a
      notice made under subsection 136(1) of the Act or conciliation by
      a request made under subsection 162(1) of the Act, the provisions
      of the Act, as amended from time to time on or after the
      commencement day, apply.

    - [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, before the commencement day, the employer or
      a bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit has given a
      notice to bargain collectively and either party has requested
      arbitration by a notice made under subsection 136(1) of the Act
      and no proceedings referred to in subsection 146(1) of the Act
      have taken place before the commencement day, the provisions of
      the Act, as amended from time to time on or after the commencement
      day, apply.

    - [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, before the commencement day, the employer or
      a bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit has given a
      notice to bargain collectively and either party has requested
      conciliation by request made under subsection 162(1) of the Act
      and no proceedings referred to in subsection 173(1) of the Act
      have taken place before the commencement day, the provisions of
      the Act, as amended from time to time on or after the commencement
      day, apply.

    - [(6)]{.lawlabel} If, before the commencement day, the employer or
      a bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit has given a
      notice to bargain and either party has requested arbitration by a
      notice made under subsection 136(1) of the Act and any proceedings
      referred to in subsection 146(1) of the Act have taken place
      before the commencement day, the provisions of the Act, as they
      read immediately before the commencement day, apply.

    - [(7)]{.lawlabel} If, before the commencement day, the employer or
      a bargaining agent representing a bargaining unit has given a
      notice to bargain and either party has requested conciliation by
      request made under subsection 162(1) of the Act and any
      proceedings referred to in subsection 173(1) of the Act have taken
      place before the commencement day, the provisions of the Act, as
      they read immediately before the commencement day, together with
      subsection 194(2) of the Act, as amended by subsection 23(2) of
      this Act, apply.

## AMENDMENTS NOT IN FORCE {#amendments-not-in-force .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2009, c. 2, s. 401, as amended by 2017, c. 9, par. 66(1)(a)

  - [401]{.lawlabel} Subsection 208(3) of the [Federal Public Sector
    Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Limitation {#limitation-3 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee may not present an individual
      grievance in respect of any matter related to equal pay for work
      of equal value or any other matter referred to in the [Public
      Sector Equitable Compensation Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65).
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2009, c. 2, s. 402, as amended by 2017, c. 9, par. 66(1)(a)

  - [402]{.lawlabel} Subsection 215(5) of the [Federal Public Sector
    Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Limitation {#limitation-4 .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} A bargaining agent may not present a group
      grievance in respect of any matter related to equal pay for work
      of equal value or any other matter referred to in the [Public
      Sector Equitable Compensation Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65).
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2009, c. 2, s. 403, as amended by 2017, c. 9, par. 66(1)(a)

  - [403]{.lawlabel} Subsection 220(3) of the [Federal Public Sector
    Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Limitation {#limitation-5 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Neither the employer nor a bargaining agent may
      present a policy grievance in respect of any matter related to
      equal pay for work of equal value or any other matter referred to
      in the [Public Sector Equitable Compensation
      Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65).
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2009, c. 2, s. 404, as amended by 2013, c. 40, s. 469(6), 2014, c.
  39, s. 385 and 2017, c. 9, par. 66(1)(a)

  - [404]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 226(2)(a) of the [Federal Public Sector
    Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} interpret and apply the [Canadian Human Rights
      Act](/eng/acts/H-6) and any other Act of Parliament relating to
      employment matters, other than the provisions of the [Canadian
      Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6) that are related to the right to
      equal pay for work of equal value and the [Public Sector Equitable
      Compensation Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65), whether or not there is a
      conflict between the Act being interpreted and applied and the
      collective agreement, if any;
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 469(1), par. (2)(b), as amended by 2017, c. 9, s.
  69(7)

  - ###### [2009, c. 2]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-2 .MarginalNote}

    - [469]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[other
      Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Budget Implementation Act,
      2009](/eng/acts/B-9.858).

    - [(2)]{.lawlabel} If section 367 of this Act comes into force
      before section 400 of the other Act, then

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on the day on which the [Public Sector
        Equitable Compensation Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65) comes into force,
        sections 13 and 14 of the second Act are replaced by the
        following:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Adjudication services {#adjudication-services .MarginalNote}

          [13]{.lawlabel} The Board is to provide adjudication services
          that consist of the hearing of applications and complaints
          made under this Part and Division 1 of Part 2.1, the referral
          of grievances to adjudication in accordance with Part 2 and
          Division 2 of Part 2.1 and the hearing of matters brought
          before the Board under Part 3. The Board is also to provide
          adjudication services in accordance with the [Public Sector
          Equitable Compensation Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65).

        - ###### Mediation services {#mediation-services .MarginalNote}

          [14]{.lawlabel} The Board is to provide mediation services
          that consist of

          - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assisting parties in the negotiation of
            collective agreements and their renewal;

          - [(b)]{.lawlabel} assisting parties in the management of the
            relations resulting from the implementation of collective
            agreements;

          - [(c)]{.lawlabel} mediating in relation to grievances; and

          - [(d)]{.lawlabel} assisting the Chairperson in discharging
            his or her responsibilities under this Act.

          The Board is also to provide mediation services in accordance
          with the [Public Sector Equitable Compensation
          Act](/eng/acts/P-31.65).
        :::
        ::::

- --- 2017, c. 9, ss. 67(1), (2)

  - ###### [2013, c. 18]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-18 .MarginalNote}

    - [67]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this section.

      [[other Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[other Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Act enacted by
          section 2 of chapter 22 of the Statutes of Canada, 2003, as
          amended from time to time. ([autre loi]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

      [[published date]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[published date]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the date
          published by the Treasury Board in the [Canada
          Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) under subsection 86(1) of
          the Enhancing Royal Canadian Mounted Police Accountability
          Act. ([date publiée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    - [(2)]{.lawlabel} If subsection 3(3) and section 30 of this Act
      come into force before the published date, then, on the published
      date,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the definition [[RCMP
        member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection 2(1) of the other Act
        is replaced by the following:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText

        [[RCMP member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

        :   [[RCMP member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned
            by the definition [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
            subsection 2(1) of the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police
            Act](/eng/acts/R-10). ([membre de la GRC]{.DefinedTermLink
            lang="fr"})
        :::
        ::::

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the portion of subsection 209(1) of the other
        Act before paragraph (a) is replaced by the following:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Reference to adjudication {#reference-to-adjudication .MarginalNote}

          - [209]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee who is not an
            RCMP member may refer to adjudication an individual
            grievance that has been presented up to and including the
            final level in the grievance process and that has not been
            dealt with to the employee's satisfaction if the grievance
            is related to
        :::
        ::::

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 238.01 of the other Act is replaced by
        the following:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Definition of [[RCMP Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} {#definition-of-rcmp-commissioner .MarginalNote}

          [238.01]{.lawlabel} In this Part, [[RCMP
          Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Commissioner of
          the Royal Canadian Mounted Police.
        :::
        ::::

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subsection 238.02(3) of the other Act is
        replaced by the following:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Clarification {#clarification-1 .MarginalNote}

          [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the provisions of
          Parts 1 and 2, in so far as they are applicable, apply to
          employees who are RCMP members or reservists unless there is
          an indication to the contrary.
        :::
        ::::

- --- 2018, c. 27, s. 431(4)

  - - [431]{.lawlabel} [(4)]{.lawlabel} Section 396 of the Act is
      repealed.
